- 2026-08-06 [Picker fix round 2] Two items. (1) Scroll/selection reset: ordering was already correct (verified — `InMemoryClipStore.fetchAll` sorts newest-first, `SearchFilter.filter` preserves order); split `PickerViewModel`'s reset logic into `selectFirstResult()` (hard reset: unconditional top-item selection + new `scrollToTopToken`) vs `reconcileSelection()` (soft: keep current selection if still visible). Hard reset fires on picker-open (`willShow()`'s query reset + the initial post-open `reload(resetSelection: true)` once fresh data lands) and on every search-text keystroke (`query`'s didSet); background live-refresh polls while open use the soft path so they don't yank the user's browsing position every ~0.4s. `PickerView` wraps the `List` in `ScrollViewReader`, scrolls to top on `scrollToTopToken` change (deferred one runloop tick via `DispatchQueue.main.async` to dodge a layout-timing race). (2) Global hotkey (T27): added `KeyboardShortcuts` dep (Sindre Sorhus, MIT). Version-verification correction worth flagging — first pass verified `v1.10.0` via `git ls-remote --tags | sort -V | tail`, pinned it, build succeeded, but `Package.resolved` came back `1.17.0` (mismatch chased, not shrugged off): the project renamed tags from `vX.Y.Z` to bare `X.Y.Z` after v1.17.0, and `sort -V` over the unfiltered combined list doesn't interleave the two formats correctly — actual latest stable is `3.0.1` (GitHub releases API confirmed), a full major ahead. Diffed Package.swift/Name.swift/Shortcut.swift between 1.x/3.x before pinning: tools-version 6.2 (fine, Xcode 26.6 = Swift 6.3.3), `default:` param renamed to non-deprecated `initial:`, `onKeyDown`/`onKeyUp` unchanged, and 3.x's `Name`/`Shortcut` are now natively `Sendable` — this actually simplified `HotkeyManager.swift` since the mistaken-1.x-pin build had genuinely failed to compile on exactly that Swift 6 strict-concurrency error, needing a `@MainActor` workaround; re-pinning to 3.x's native `Sendable` removed the need for it. Pinned `from: 3.0.1`, `Package.resolved` confirms exact resolution. New `ClipnestApp/Sources/System/HotkeyManager.swift`: `KeyboardShortcuts.Name.togglePicker` defaults to ⌥⌘V (Option+Command+V, per explicit instruction overriding the spec's ⌘⇧V — "set on first launch if unset" is the library's own `Name.init` behavior, no bespoke bookkeeping added), `HotkeyManager.register(onToggle:)` thin wrapper (no pure logic to unit-test, documented manual-verification requirement instead); `AppEnvironment.registerHotkey()` wires it to the same `showPicker()` path the menu item uses, called from `applicationDidFinishLaunching`. Clean rebuild (network-resolving the new dep, as expected) → `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, `Clipnest.app` present. New tests: `setPinnedSetsAndClearsPinnedAt()` + `pinUnpinRepinCycleUpdatesPinnedAt()`, one matching pair in each of `ClipStoreTests`/`SwiftDataClipStoreTests` — asserted presence/absence of `pinnedAt` rather than strict timestamp ordering (no injectable clock on `setPinned`, and adding one wasn't asked for). `swift build`/`swift test` (root) → 117/117 (113 + 4 new). `xcodegen generate` clean; `xcodebuild ... Documented, not engineered against: self-write-suppression's write-to-suppress gap grew from near-instant to up to ~40ms in the synthesis-granted path only; ~400ms ClipboardMonitor poll interval keeps the race odds low but no longer negligible — flagged as accepted minor risk rather than restructuring Paster's API for a rare edge case. - 2026-08-10 [T47] User-reported perf bug: "app hangs terribly while searching." Root-caused (not guessed) with a standalone benchmark before touching code: `PickerViewModel`'s History/Pinned `items` was 4 chained, uncached computed properties (`filteredItems`→`pinnedRun`/`unpinnedRun`→`items`), each re-running `SearchFilter.filter` over the *entire* `allItems` array on every read, synchronously on @MainActor, with zero debounce — and `PickerView`'s body reads `items` 2-3x per render, so one keystroke triggered 2-3 full O(n) scans back-to-back blocking the run loop. Confirmed the actual trigger: `ClipItem.previewText` has no length cap (`.text`/`.link` store the *full* content there — it doubles as what gets pasted back), so a single realistic large paste (a copied source file/log, ~2MB) pushed one filter pass from ~2ms to 30-70ms in my benchmark (`localizedCaseInsensitiveContains` over a large string) — paid 2-3x per keystroke, no debounce, reproducing "hangs terribly" exactly. Fix (`PickerViewModel.swift` only): collapsed the 4 computed properties into one `@Published private(set) var items` recomputed by a single `scheduleFilterUpdate(_:debounced:)` pipeline — the actual filter/sort pass now runs inside `Task.detached` (off @MainActor, via a new `nonisolated static filteredAndSorted(...)`), never inline during a SwiftUI render; only the final `self.items = result` + selection update touch @MainActor (via `MainActor.run`). Debounce (~120ms) applied ONLY to the search-text-typing path (`query`'s didSet); tab switches, live-refresh polls, and post-mutation reloads (pin/delete) all stay immediate/non-debounced, unchanged from before. `filterTask` is a single cancellable property (latest-request-wins, `Task.isCancelled` re-checked after every suspension point) so overlapping calls (e.g. a keystroke landing mid-poll) never stale-overwrite a newer result. `selectFirstResult()`/`reconcileSelection()`/`highlightedItem`/`moveSelection`/etc. needed ZERO changes — they only ever read `items`, and a stored property satisfies that identically to the old computed one; only `query`'s/`activeTab`'s didSets and the two `reload(...)` overloads changed their call site. Traced every interleaving (willShow's debounced reset vs startLiveRefresh's immediate reload racing on open, delete's select-near-neighbor vs a concurrent keystroke) by hand to confirm no regression — no App-level test target exists for this file (established precedent, D18-D22, T43) so this is build+lint+manual-trace verified, not unit-tested; flagged, not silently skipped. Deliberately did NOT touch the second, compounding cause found during root-causing: `ClipStore.fetchAll` has no result cap and re-fetches the *entire* history table every ~0.4s poll — capping that (or defaulting T32's retention cap on) is a product/perf trade-off (how far back History can search), not a pure bugfix; flagging as a follow-up for architect/PM rather than changing unilaterally. `xcodegen generate` + `xcodebuild -project ClipnestApp/ClipnestApp.xcodeproj -scheme ClipnestApp -configuration Debug -derivedDataPath ClipnestApp/DerivedData CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build` → `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, zero warnings on the touched file. `swift test` → 131/131 unchanged (App-layer-only change, zero `ClipnestCore` touched). `swift format lint --recursive --strict ClipnestApp/Sources Sources Tests` clean. Decision + benchmark numbers recorded as D23 in project-context.md. `git add` done on the one touched file, no commit. → board:review (my ceiling here — no reviewer/tester pass run this turn); handing to reviewer then tester next. Runtime-only check (typing in search genuinely feels instant/no-lag now, especially after a large paste; selection/highlight still correct across rapid typing, tab switches, and delete-during-search) still needed from the human — the benchmark proves the mechanism, not the on-screen feel.

- 2026-08-10 [T47 review-fix] Addressed reviewer's `perf-review.md` findings on T47's search-perf fix, same one file (`PickerViewModel.swift`), no scope creep. Finding 1 (Important, blocking): commit actions (Return/⌘P/⌘S/⌘⌫) could act on a row up to ~120ms stale relative to the just-typed query during the debounce window — wrong-paste/wrong-delete hazard. Fixed with new `flushPendingFilter()` (cancels any pending debounced `filterTask`, synchronously recomputes `items`/selection from the current query via new `applyFilterSynchronously(_:)`), called at the top of `selectHighlighted()`/`togglePinHighlighted()`/`saveHighlightedAsSnippet()`/`deleteHighlighted()`. - 2026-08-11 [T53 — critical launch-crash fix] Confirmed from a real runtime log: T49/T50's `normalizedText` (added non-optional, no default) failed SwiftData lightweight migration of the user's real, intact on-disk store (`NSCocoaErrorDomain 134110`) — `ModelContainer` construction threw, `AppEnvironment.init` propagated it, `AppDelegate` terminated on every launch. Fix, two files: `ClipItemRecord.normalizedText`/`SnippetRecord.normalizedText` → `String = ""` (SwiftData only auto-migrates a new non-optional attribute if it's defaulted — mirrors Core Data's lightweight-migration rule); both stores' `init` now run a new private static `backfillNormalizedText(in:)` against their `ModelContext` before assigning `self.*` — fetches rows where `normalizedText == ""` and the source text isn't (bounded `#Predicate`, not a full scan), sets it to the exact same derivation new inserts already use (`previewText.lowercased()` / `(title + " " + body).lowercased()`), one batched `save()`; runs every launch but only does real work once; failures logged (metadata only) and swallowed, never thrown — a backfill hiccup must not reintroduce the crash. - 2026-08-11 [T55 — search debounce + loader] Fixed the reported picker-search-lags-behind-typing bug: even after D29/T54's focus-race fix, the search `TextField` still bound straight to `viewModel.query.text`, and `query`'s `didSet` drove the debounced store query — so a keystroke only appeared once its async DB round-trip resolved (up to 400ms + store latency), the standard "bound the input to the debounce" anti-pattern. Retargeted to the standard pattern: `PickerView` gained a local `@State searchText` the field binds directly (zero async in the input path, always instant); `.onChange(of: searchText)` calls new `PickerViewModel.searchTextChanged(_:)`, which records it into new `currentSearchText`, flips new `isSearching` on, and (re)starts the existing 400ms debounce via a new shared `runActiveTabQuery(policy:debounced:)` dispatcher (reused T50/T51's debounce/generation-counter machinery, extended with an explicit `text:` param instead of always reading `query.text`). `query.text` repurposed to mean "the text a query actually ran for" — written in exactly one place (once `runRowsFirstPageQuery`/`runSnippetsFirstPageQuery` lands) — so `ItemRow`/`SnippetRow` highlighting and the empty-state message (unchanged call sites) always match what's shown, never a keystroke in flight; `query`'s `didSet` now only reacts to `kindFilter`. New `isSearching` drives a centered `ProgressView()` loader in `PickerView.content`, replacing list/empty-state while a search-driven query (typing/tab-switch/kind-filter) is in flight — deliberately not flipped for live-capture/mutation refreshes (out of scope, already have non-jarring selection policies). Self-review caught + closed two real races before reviewer: (1) `willShow()`'s `currentSearchText` reset lands synchronously but `PickerView`'s own `searchText` reset (new `searchResetToken`) lands one SwiftUI cycle later and would otherwise re-fire `searchTextChanged` and flash the loader on every reopen-after-a-search — closed with a `text != currentSearchText` guard; (2) tab switches only cancelled the newly-active pipeline's task, leaving a stray debounce on the abandoned tab free to wake later and prematurely clear `isSearching` for the tab now on screen — closed by having `runActiveTabQuery` cancel the inactive pipeline's task too. `flushPendingRowsQuery`/`flushPendingSnippetsQuery`/`handleNewCapture` updated to read `currentSearchText` (the live field text) instead of the now-lagging `query.text`. Focus behavior (D29/T54) untouched — typing being local `@State` makes it structurally impossible for the async pipeline to interfere with keystroke acceptance at all. Confirmed root cause empirically first (isolated repro script before touching code): `hdiutil attach ... -quiet` emits zero stdout, so the mount-path `grep -o '/Volumes/...'` always failed (exit 1), and under `set -euo pipefail` that killed the script at the bare `MNT="$(...)"` assignment — before the scripts' own `if [ -z "${MNT}" ]; then echo error; exit 1; fi` guard ever ran. Added end-of-script success verification: `install.sh` asserts `/Applications/Clipnest.app` exists + prints installed version in its final line; `update.sh` asserts the same plus that post-copy version equals the release's `LATEST` tag before printing `Updated to X.` **Tested for real end-to-end** (the routing brief's explicit ask, since `bash -n`-only testing is what shipped this bug): `bash scripts/install.sh` against the live v0.6.0 GitHub release — downloaded/mounted/installed, confirmed `ls -ld /Applications/Clipnest.app` exists and `CFBundleShortVersionString` = `0.6.0`, ended with a non-silent `Done. - 2026-09-03 [T-GLY1 — glyph/emoji classifier spike, GATE FAILED, T-GLY2 NOT started] Built the full spike per brief: standalone SPM package `tools/glyph-classifier/` (never referenced by root `Package.swift`/`ClipnestApp/project.yml` — keeps `CreateML` and the AppKit/CoreText renderer entirely out of shipping targets), 3 targets (`GlyphDatasetGen` renders synthetic data, `GlyphTrainer` trains+evaluates via `CreateML.MLImageClassifier`, `GlyphClassifierSupport` is the shared class catalog + pure gate-evaluation logic, unit-tested — 8/8 `swift test` green in that package). Classes: 9 keyboard glyphs (⌘⌥⇧⌃⎋⏎⌫⇥⇪, added ⇥/⇪ beyond the brief's list, hand-verified real font glyph coverage per font via `CTFontGetGlyphsForCharacters` before committing to the renderer), 64 emoji (single-codepoint, non-ZWJ, non-skin-tone, spread across 7 categories — stated cut: kept small enough for a same-session spike, not a frequency-ranked corpus pull), 1 `notSymbol` (ASCII+confusables+partial-text-crops+blank-crops, deliberately the largest class). Generated 15,870 PNGs (179MB) in 12.8s with heavy randomized augmentation (font/weight/size/color incl. dark-mode+low-contrast pairs/rotation/noise/blur/crop-looseness). Trained `MLImageClassifier` (`.scenePrint` transfer learning + logistic regressor, `augmentation: []` since the renderer already varies heavily) — 287s wall time, output model 1.2MB (1,198,579 bytes; feature extractor stays OS-provided, not embedded).
  **Held-out SYNTHETIC test (self-consistent with the renderer, not a real-world proxy):** overall accuracy 93.54%; glyph-group micro-recall 82.22% (worst: capslock 60%/precision 54.5%, ctrl 64%/76.2%, shift 76%/77.6% — capslock/shift are near-identical up-arrow shapes in several fonts, a plausible root cause); emoji-group micro-recall 96.75%; `notSymbol` precision 93.151%, recall 93.500%. **Gate on synthetic alone already FAILS both criteria** (notSymbol precision needs ≥99.5%, glyph recall needs ≥90%).
  **Real-world crops (the number that actually matters — mid-task course correction from the coordinator required expanding this from an initial thin n=5 set):** queried `~/Library/Application Support/Clipnest/ClipItems.store` (read-only, `ZKINDRAWVALUE='image'`) for the authoritative list of 39 real image blobs; ran the SAME production-shaped Vision `.accurate` pass (1600pt downscale) against all 39, harvested 480 real per-character/word `notSymbol` crops (visually spot-checked, all correctly real text — "agent"/"Generic"/digits/"×"/"←"/etc.) plus every glyph-shaped candidate token found (53 candidates → only ONE real isolated ⌘ occurrence exists anywhere in this user's 39-image corpus, in a single Clipnest-docs screenshot; zero real occurrences of the other 8 glyph classes; zero real emoji occurrences anywhere). **Real `notSymbol` recall: 66.875% (321/480)** — roughly 1 in 3 real characters/words from actual screenshots get mis-rewritten into a glyph or emoji, 114/480 (23.75%) specifically into `cmd` alone. **Real `cmd` recall: 0/2** (the only two real ⌘ crops found were both misclassified as `notSymbol`) — the model fails in both directions on real data. Real `notSymbol` precision is not honestly computable from this corpus (too few real glyph/emoji occurrences exist to estimate a fair denominator) — stated explicitly rather than reporting a misleadingly-high number. Root cause (not just "more data needed"): Vision's per-character `boundingBox(for:)` is NOT reliably tight to one character on real content — confirmed twice (a merged ⌥⌘ token returned identical boxes for two different character offsets; several harvested "single-character" real crops came back showing whole words) — so a classifier trained exclusively on tightly-cropped single symbols hits a real domain/crop-shape gap that heavier synthetic augmentation alone likely can't close.
  **Latency:** 12.46ms/prediction (synthetic), 29.02ms/prediction (real crops) — both far under Vision's 154ms `.accurate` baseline for one image. Per-screenshot cost estimate: production would only classify short suspicious tokens (never blindly classify every recognized character, per the brief); this harvest's own candidate-token heuristic found ~1.4 candidates/real-image, so ~20-40ms added per screenshot if restricted that way — negligible; blindly classifying every character instead (hundreds per dense screenshot) would NOT be negligible (multi-second), confirming the brief's explicit warning against that design.
  **GATE: FAIL — clearly, on both synthetic and (far more severely) real data.** This is the harmful "worse than nothing" failure mode the brief named directly: shipping this would silently corrupt ~1/3 of real notSymbol characters near any suspicious-looking region. **T-GLY2 (integration) was NOT started** — no Phase 2 code, no `computeNormalizedText` change, no schema change, per the brief's explicit instruction to stop and report a negative result rather than integrate a weak model or quietly lower the bar. `CFStringTransform(_, nil, "Any-Name" as CFString, false)` was verified as the real system API for Phase 2's emoji-alias derivation (e.g. 🚀 → `\N{ROCKET}`), recorded for whoever revisits this, not used in any shipped code.
  Zero files touched outside `tools/glyph-classifier/` and `.gitignore` (new gitignore entries for that package's `.build/`/`dataset/`/`output/`/`real_eval_crops/` — the last holds crops from the user's real, sensitive screenshots, never committed). Confirmed no regression: root `swift test` → **212/212** unchanged; `swift format lint --recursive --strict Sources Tests` and `... ClipnestApp/Sources` → clean (pre-existing `HotkeyManagerTests.swift:25` untouched); `tools/glyph-classifier`'s own `swift format lint --recursive --strict Sources Tests` → clean; its own `swift test` → 8/8. `xcodebuild ... test` (104-test app baseline) run to confirm no regression from this task — see final report for its result (was still running at log time). No dependency added to any shipping target (`CreateML`/`Vision`-for-harvesting/`TabularData` are confined to `tools/glyph-classifier/`, a non-shipping tool package — nothing added to `Sources/ClipnestCore` or `ClipnestApp`'s actual import surface). Coding-standards.md needs no update (no shipping-target change). Was spawned this session (senior-dev role) — returning status/decisions to my spawner rather than writing `.claude/task-board.md` (integrity rule 1); T-GLY1's furthest honest status is `test` for the spike's own code (dataset generator + trainer + their unit tests work correctly and are lint-clean), but the FEATURE it was gating (T-GLY2) should be recorded as **not started, blocked by this negative gate result** — recommend the board record T-GLY1 as measured/failed rather than a normal `done`, and NOT create/advance T-GLY2 until a product decision is made on whether to revisit with a different approach (e.g. training on Vision-shaped multi-character crops instead of tight single-glyph crops, or dropping the ML approach in favor of the existing "explain what Vision can't read" status quo).
- 2026-09-03 [T-PERF1 — get heavy work off the main actor, per user directive] Enforced one rule end-to-end: **AppKit pasteboard access stays on `@MainActor`; nothing else heavy does.** Two hot paths, both fixed at the root rather than patched with `Task { @MainActor in }` band-aids.
  **Capture path** (`Sources/ClipnestCore/Clipboard/PasteboardReader.swift`, `ClipboardMonitor.swift`): split `PasteboardReader.read(from:)` into `pullRawPayload(from:) -> RawPayload?` (the real `NSPasteboard` reads — `availableTypes`/`data(forType:)`/`string(forType:)` — fetches ONLY the one representation the existing type-priority order resolves to, exactly like `read` always did) and `classify(_ raw: RawPayload) -> Classification` (pure CPU: `ItemKind` decision, `previewText` construction, SHA-256 hashing — touches no `PasteboardReading`). `RawPayload` is a small `Sendable` enum (`.file`/`.image`/`.richText`/`.plainText`) carrying exactly the bytes/strings each branch used to read inline. `read(from:)` itself is unchanged (`pullRawPayload` + `classify` combined) so `PasteboardReaderTests` needed zero changes. `ClipboardMonitor.checkNow()` now calls `pullRawPayload` on `@MainActor` (unchanged), then `classify` inside `Task.detached(priority: .utility)` (same pattern its blob write already used) — `checkNow()` tests needed zero signature changes since it was already `async`. Also switched `imagePreviewText`'s dimension read from `NSBitmapImageRep(data:)` to `ImageIO`'s `CGImageSourceCopyPropertiesAtIndex` (header-only read, no full decode, and a documented-thread-safe C API instead of an AppKit type — matters now that `classify` runs off `@MainActor`).
  **Paste path**: (1) `ClipnestApp/Sources/UI/Picker/PickerViewModel+Paste.swift` — `pasteContent(for:plainText:)` is now `async`; `.richText`/`.image` blob reads go through a new `readBlobOffMain(_:)` (`Task.detached(priority: .utility) { blobStore.read(...) }` — same pattern as `ItemPreview`/`ItemRow`/`ClipboardMonitor`'s existing off-main blob reads). `select(_:plainText:)` restructured: its `Task` now resolves `pasteContent` FIRST, and only THEN captures `frontmostApp`/dismisses — preserves the exact original behavior that a `nil` content (missing/corrupt blob) leaves the picker open with nothing pasted (never dismiss-then-no-op). The old shared `pasteAndDismiss(_:)` (captured `frontmostApp` + dismissed + pasted, all in one) split into: callers (`select`/`pasteSnippet`) each capture `frontmostApp`/dismiss themselves (immediately, synchronously — `pasteSnippet` has no I/O so this is unchanged for it), then both call the still-shared `performPaste(_:frontmostApp:) async` for the write-and-suppress tail. (2) `Sources/ClipnestCore/Paste/Paster.swift` — `.image`'s `NSImage(data:)?.tiffRepresentation` decode+re-encode (measured ~83ms combined on the user's 25MB real image) replaced with a `private static func normalizedToTIFF(_ data:) -> Data?` built entirely on `ImageIO` (`CGImageSourceCreateWithData`/`CGImageSourceCreateImageAtIndex`/`CGImageDestinationCreateWithData`/`...AddImage`/`...Finalize`, output type `UTType.tiff`), called via `Task.detached(priority: .utility)` from inside `paste()`'s `.image` case — chose `Task.detached` (not relying on the caller having already hopped off-main) specifically because it's a hard guarantee regardless of what actor invoked `paste(_:targetingFrontmostApp:)`, unlike depending on every future call site getting an off-main hop right. Same `PasteError.invalidImageData` contract on decode/encode failure as before.
  **Why `changeCount`/self-paste-suppression ordering is still correct** (hazard 1): the real invariant `suppressOwnPasteboardWrite`'s callers depend on is "the pasteboard write completes before `paster.paste(...)` returns to its `await`er" — NOT "no suspension before the write inside `paste`" (that was just how the old synchronous-decode implementation happened to satisfy it). Swift's sequential-execution guarantee for a single async call already gives the stronger property for free: no matter how many suspension points happen *before* the write inside `paste()`, the write is still guaranteed complete before `paste()` returns, since execution proceeds statement-by-statement to the return. So `performPaste`'s `suppressOwnPasteboardWrite(pasteboard.changeCount)`, read immediately after `await paster.paste(...)` returns, still observes the correct value. The actual race this codebase's comments warn about — the window between the write and `suppressOwnPasteboardWrite`'s `ignore(changeCount:)` registration racing the monitor's 400ms poll — is **unchanged**: decode-before-write was already the ordering pre-T-PERF1 (just synchronous instead of suspended), so nothing moved earlier relative to the write; the new `Task.detached` for blob-read/decode only delays *when* the write happens overall (giving the OS *more* time to hand focus back before `dismiss()`+`synthesisDelay`, not less) — the write-to-suppress window itself is untouched.
  **Why `PrivacyFilter` gate ordering is still correct** (hazard 2): `checkNow()`'s ordering is unchanged — `privacyFilter.shouldCapture(...)` (using only `pasteboard.availableTypes`, a cheap main-actor read) still runs and can still reject BEFORE `pullRawPayload`/`classify`/blob-write/store-insert/OCR are ever reached; I only touched what happens strictly after that gate already passed.
  **`ClipboardMonitor` state** (hazard 3): `lastChangeCount`/`ignoredChangeCount` mutations are untouched — both still happen synchronously at the top of `checkNow()`, before the new `await Task.detached { classify }` suspension point I added; only the *work* (classification) moved, the state-machine ordering didn't.
  **Swift 6 strict concurrency**: zero new `@unchecked Sendable` anywhere in the touched production files (verified via `git diff | grep "unchecked Sendable"` — none). `RawPayload` is a plain `Sendable` enum of `Data`/`String`. `PasteboardReader`/`Paster` are already plain `Sendable` structs, so capturing them into `Task.detached` closures needs no escape hatch. Test-only `@unchecked Sendable` reference types (`Counter`, in the new `PasterTests` concurrency test) mirror the exact same pattern this codebase's tests already use (`CaptureFailureRecorder`, `MutableBox` in `ClipboardMonitorTests.swift`) — single-threaded-by-construction, standard for a test-local mutable box.
  **Verify, structural + empirical**: added `classify(_:)` genuinely runs off the main thread when called from Task.detached` (`PasteboardReaderTests.swift`) — `Task.detached { reader.classify(raw) }` compiles with no `await` needed on `classify` itself (a `classify` secretly requiring `@MainActor` would not compile there), PLUS a runtime `Thread.isMainThread` check (wrapped in a plain sync helper since the property itself is `NS_SWIFT_UNAVAILABLE_FROM_ASYNC`) confirms it's genuinely not on the main thread. Also added a byte-identical-output regression test (`pullRawPayload` + `classify` == `read(from:)`). Added `` `.image` decode+re-encode does not block the calling MainActor`` (`PasterTests.swift`) — a `@MainActor` counter task races alongside `paste(.image(2000×2000 synthetic))`; asserts `counter.value > 0` (loose, non-fragile — proves genuine interleaving happened, not an exact timing bound; would read `0` against the old synchronous-decode code). **Real measurement** against the user's actual 25MB blob (`d8370438...850f4f2`, confirmed present, 25,273,086 bytes — read-only throughout, `BlobStore.read` only, never `write`, via a throwaway test file I deleted before finishing): off-main blob read 9–101ms (machine/disk-cache-variance across 4 runs, all off-main), full `Paster.paste(.image:)` (ImageIO decode+encode off-main + pasteboard write on-main) 95–200ms wall-clock across runs on this dev machine's debug (`-Onone`) build (noisier than the user's original 34+49+11≈94ms figure, consistent with `-Onone` overhead — **not directly comparable to the user's release-adjacent number**, so I'm not claiming a speedup, only that the same work now runs off-main), and a concurrent `@MainActor` heartbeat prober (1ms `Task.sleep` ticks) measured max gaps of 9–23ms during that window — small relative to total wall time, though not zero (attributable to `Task.sleep` scheduling granularity under a debug build, not to the decode blocking the main actor, per the deterministic counter-task test above which is the harder proof). Baseline (root `swift test`) went 212→**215/215** (+2 `PasteboardReaderTests`, +1 `PasterTests`); App (`xcodegen generate` + `xcodebuild ... -destination 'platform=macOS' CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO test`) stayed **104/104, 11 suites, TEST SUCCEEDED** (19 existing `PickerViewModelPasteContentTests` funcs updated to `async`/`await` for the now-`async` `pasteContent`, zero new App tests, zero behavior assertions changed). `swift format lint --recursive --strict Sources Tests ClipnestApp/Sources` → clean; `swift format lint --strict ClipnestApp/Tests` → only the known pre-existing `HotkeyManagerTests.swift:25` LineLength failure (confirmed pre-dating this task, left alone per instruction) — every file I touched individually re-linted clean. - 2026-09-03 [T-HANG1/T-HANG2] Fixed the reproduced paste hang (P0) + the self-paste-suppression race, both confirmed root-caused by T-STRESS1. Independently re-reproduced BOTH before touching code: (a) ran `tools/stress-harness` fresh, hit the same section-7 stall, `sample` on my own run (`.claude/logs/stress-artifacts/senior-dev-before-fix-sample1.txt`) shows 9 cooperative-pool threads pinned in `ClipboardMonitor.scheduleTextRecognition`→`VisionTextRecognizer.recognize`→`withCheckedContinuation`, zero `Paster` frames anywhere — matches tester's `sample_paste_hang.txt` exactly; (b) wrote a NEW deterministic unit-level regression test (`VisionTextRecognizerTests.manyConcurrentRecognitionsDoNotStarveTheCooperativePool`) that reproduces the same starvation in `swift test` itself — watched it FAIL against the unfixed code (killed at 6:21/381s elapsed, only 4.35s CPU consumed — genuinely blocked, not spinning; `sample` on the stuck test process shows 13 cooperative threads pinned in `recognizeText`, the racing heartbeat's `tick()` essentially starved — 1 sample hit in a 3s capture window).

T-HANG1 root cause (own words): two compounding defects in `Sources/ClipnestCore/OCR/VisionTextRecognizer.swift`. (1) `recognize(in:quality:)` called the SYNCHRONOUS, blocking `VNImageRequestHandler.perform(_:)` directly inside a `withCheckedContinuation` closure with no `await` inside — the textbook blocking-work-in-async-context anti-pattern, which pins whatever Swift-concurrency cooperative-pool thread runs it for the call's full duration. (2) `ClipboardMonitor.scheduleTextRecognition` fired one uncapped `Task.detached(priority:.utility)` per `.image` capture — N images captured together meant N cooperative-pool threads blocked at once; on this 10-core machine ~10 images fully pinned the pool, starving ALL other off-main work app-wide, including `Paster.paste(.image:)`'s own detached TIFF-normalize step (measured stall: 164s+, never resolved, in T-STRESS1's original repro).

Fix: added `VisionTextRecognizer.recognitionQueue`, a dedicated SERIAL `DispatchQueue` (`qos: .utility`) that Swift concurrency doesn't own. `recognizeText(in:quality:)` now dispatches its ENTIRE pipeline (decode, pixel-ceiling check, downscale, the `VNRecognizeTextRequest`/`perform` call) onto this queue via `.async` inside the `withCheckedContinuation` closure — no cooperative-pool thread is ever blocked by Vision again, at any load. Being SERIAL also structurally enforces the concurrency cap with no separate counter needed. Cap chosen: `maxConcurrentRecognitions = 1`, justified not just as "safe" but as throughput-OPTIMAL: the `sample` evidence itself shows Apple's own `VNDetector` already serializes the real work onto its own internal serial queue (`com.apple.VN.detectorSyncTasksQueue.VNCRImageReaderDetector`) regardless of caller concurrency — a cap of 2+ would only occupy a second thread for zero additional throughput. Queueing policy: FIFO (GGD's default for a serial queue), unbounded depth, nothing ever dropped — a burst just makes that image's OCR latency go up, never silently loses a requested recognition; `checkNow()` capture latency is completely unaffected either way since OCR scheduling was always fire-and-forget. Regression test switched from an initial (flawed) "outstanding-calls" tracker — which can't distinguish "queued" from "genuinely executing" once ANY queue exists, so it never went green even against the fix — to a cooperative-pool-starvation proof (races an unrelated sleep-paced heartbeat `Task.detached` against N=`activeProcessorCount` concurrent `.fast` recognitions, asserts worst tick gap stays under 1000ms). Watched fail (381s+, killed) then pass (0.582s) against the identical test, same machine.

T-HANG2 root cause (own words): `PickerViewModel+Paste.performPaste` called `suppressOwnPasteboardWrite(pasteboard.changeCount)` only AFTER `await paster.paste(...)` had FULLY returned — i.e. after the write, then `synthesisDelay` (40ms default) elapsed, then a real event post. `ClipboardMonitor.ignore(changeCount:)` was therefore armed 40ms+ after the write, a window the real 0.4s capture poll could and did land inside (T-STRESS1: 18/18 raced at 5-42ms; for `.image` content, `Paster`'s TIFF re-encode isn't byte-identical to the original so a raced self-paste computed a genuinely different `contentHash` — a real duplicate row + wasted blob, not just a bumped `createdAt`).

Fix: `Paster.paste(_:targetingFrontmostApp:onPasteboardWrite:)` gained a new `onPasteboardWrite: (@MainActor @Sendable (Int) -> Void)? = nil` parameter (defaults nil, every other/test call site unaffected), invoked with `pasteboard.changeCount` immediately after the write — before `synthesisDelay`'s sleep even starts. `@MainActor`-typed (not `Paster` itself, which stays actor-agnostic) because its one real caller needs to call a `@MainActor` method; `paste()` `await`s it (a single actor hop, not a 40ms sleep). `PickerViewModel+Paste.performPaste` now passes `suppressOwnPasteboardWrite` straight through as this callback instead of reading `pasteboard.changeCount` itself after `paste()` returns — the post-call read+suppress line is gone entirely. Updated `Paster.paste`'s doc comment (the invariant callers depend on, and the "adding a suspension before the write is safe / between write-and-report is not" reasoning) to state the new contract. Regression test in `PasterTests.swift` (`onPasteboardWriteFiresBeforeSynthesizedKeystroke`, deterministic, `synthesisDelay: .zero`, proves call ORDER via a recorder — not wall-clock timing, since this file's convention forbids real timers): watched it fail (temporarily moved the call to after the synthesizer, exactly reproducing the OLD ordering — `events == ["synthesized", "wrote:42"]`), then pass after restoring. Two more new tests: `onPasteboardWriteFiresForImageContent` (proves the callback still fires with the right changeCount even with `.image`'s internal `Task.detached` decode suspension before the write) and `onPasteboardWriteNotCalledWhenWriteNeverHappens` (invalid image data throws before any write — callback correctly never fires).

Updated `tools/stress-harness/Sources/StressHarness/Scenarios/SelfPasteRaceScenario.swift`'s `raceTrial` to mirror the NEW production ordering (`onPasteboardWrite` fires before the sleep, not after `paste()` returns) and tightened the offset sweep to sub-ms/low-ms (was: straddling the 40ms boundary). Fresh harness run (`.claude/logs/stress-artifacts/senior-dev-after-fix-run.txt`): TEXT 0/24 self-captured at every offset from 0ms to 80ms (was 18/18 raced at 5-42ms); IMAGE 0/18 self-captured at every offset 0-40ms (was 2/3 raced at 55ms); `imageRaceProducedDistinctContentHash` now `false` (was `true`). Section 7 (paste-during-OCR-saturation, the exact scenario that stalled 164s+ unresolved before) now completes cleanly: `ocrImagesSubmitted=14 baselinePaste=0.7ms duringSaturationPaste=0.2ms slowdown=0.3x`, full harness reaches "DONE" for the first time this project has seen.

Honest caveat found and NOT hidden: the harness's own `ConcurrencyTracker`/`maxConcurrentRecognitions` metric (used by `OCRConcurrencyScenario`) still reports "6 of 6" post-fix — this is a real limitation of that specific metric, not evidence the fix doesn't work: it brackets `enter()`/`exit()` around the WHOLE `recognizeText` call (submission to completion), so a call sitting queued behind the new serial `recognitionQueue` looks identical to one genuinely executing — both count as "outstanding." Did not fix the harness's tracker (out of this task's scope, harness isn't production code) — flagging it so nobody cites "6 of 6" as post-fix counter-evidence; the real proof is the starvation/hang tests above, which measure the actual symptom, not outstanding-call count.

T-SET1 (Settings window opens behind other apps, macOS 26) — reproduced independently before touching code: focused Terminal, triggered "Settings…" via the AX tree, confirmed `osascript` frontmost stayed "Terminal" and `CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo` showed Clipnest's Settings window ("Shortcuts") on-screen but at index 2, behind 2 other real windows; debug log showed `openSettingsAndFocus: raising window, appActive=false` — matches the routed evidence exactly. Root cause confirmed: `.accessory`-policy apps have no Dock icon, and macOS will not select/activate a window belonging to a Dock-icon-less app on this OS — no amount of `orderFrontRegardless`/`makeKeyAndOrderFront`/`activate(ignoringOtherApps:)` changes that; round 1's fix (RunLoop deferral + those calls) was necessary but not sufficient. Independently corroborated via web search: Peter Steinberger's "Showing Settings from macOS Menu Bar Items" (steipete.me, Jun 2025) documents the identical failure on macOS Tahoe (26) and ships the same fix in production (VibeTunnel).

Fix: new `ClipnestApp/Sources/App/SettingsActivator.swift` — briefly flips `NSApp.setActivationPolicy(.regular)`, force-activates (`activate(ignoringOtherApps: true)`, kept over cooperative `NSApp.activate()`/`yieldActivation(to:)` — evaluated and rejected, see the type's doc comment for why), then restores `.accessory` via a one-shot `NSWindow.willCloseNotification` observer on the Settings window — never on a timer, never immediately after activating. Wired into `MenuBarContent.openSettingsAndFocus()` (`ClipnestApp.swift`) as a `private static let` (not instance state — `MenuBarContent` is a SwiftUI `View` struct recreated on nearly every menu render, so instance state would silently lose "are we currently `.regular`"). Live verification caught a real bug in my first version: the idempotency guard I used to avoid double-registering the close observer also skipped force-activation on a second "Settings…" click while the window was already open but had lost focus (user switched to another app without closing Settings) — a second click did nothing. Fixed by decoupling: `setActivationPolicy`/the close-observer registration stay guarded (once per open/close cycle), `activateApp()` now runs unconditionally on every `activate(observing:)` call (harmless when already active). All AppKit calls are injected via closures so the sequencing/guard logic has unit coverage (`SettingsActivatorTests.swift`, 5 tests: first-open runs full sequence, repeat-open-while-open re-activates without re-toggling policy/double-observing — the regression test for the bug above, close restores accessory, double-close-fire only restores once, reopen-after-close re-arms the full sequence). The AppKit calls themselves are not unit-testable (no window server in `swift test`) — verified manually only, per below. Fixed one new Swift 6 strict-concurrency warning I introduced (captured-var-mutated-after-capture on the notification token) using the same `nonisolated(unsafe)`-boxed-reference-type justification already established by `PickerPanel.commandDeleteMonitor`, plus `MainActor.assumeIsolated` at the `queue: .main` boundary matching `HotkeyManager.observeLibraryNotifications()`'s existing pattern — zero new warnings from my files (confirmed against a stashed pre-change baseline: the `RunLoop.main.perform(inModes:)` MainActor-isolation warnings in `ClipnestApp.swift` are 100% pre-existing, same count/shape before and after).

Manual verification on this machine (macOS 26.6.2), Accessibility tree + `CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo` (screencapture is TCC-blocked) — reproduced fail, then re-verified fixed after every code change: (1) fresh open from another app → frontmost flips to Clipnest, Settings window rises above every real app window (only ahead of a persistent sandbox-harness overlay ["Terminal Hub"/hub-app] that sits above everything in this dev environment regardless of activation policy, before and after — not a Clipnest window, not affected by this fix). (2) Dock icon appears for as long as Settings stays open (this is a real, non-flash, visible Dock icon — not a brief flicker — same tradeoff steipete's writeup documents; disclosing plainly per the task's ask rather than hiding it) and disappears within ~1-2s of closing (small Dock un-bounce lag, not a bug — window itself is confirmed fully closed immediately). (3) Reclaim-focus case: switch to another app while Settings stays open, click "Settings…" again → Clipnest reclaims frontmost (this is the case my first implementation failed and the second fixed). (4) Picker unaffected: triggered "Open Clipnest" while Finder frontmost → Finder stays frontmost, Clipnest shows no Dock icon — `PickerPanel`'s non-activating behavior and `FrontmostAppTracker`'s paste-target recording are untouched by this change (`SettingsActivator` is never invoked from the picker's code path). One accepted, pre-existing-class edge case not introduced by this fix: if the user has Settings open (now genuinely frontmost) and invokes the picker via hotkey before closing it, `FrontmostAppTracker` would record Clipnest itself as the paste target — identical in kind to the already-existing `SnippetEditorWindow` (a real activating window), not a new regression, out of this task's scope.

- 2026-09-04 [T-SET5] User report "cmd+, isn't working properly" for Settings — was true, ⌘, had never been wired anywhere. Built it for the two approved contexts: while the picker is open, and while Settings itself is focused. Reused T-SET1's activation machinery end-to-end rather than building a second path — extracted `MenuBarContent.openSettingsAndFocus()`'s window-finding/`SettingsActivator`-driving/RunLoop-deferral sequence into new `ClipnestApp/Sources/App/SettingsFocusCoordinator.swift` (`focusAfterOpening()`), called from both `MenuBarContent` and the picker's new path; `MenuBarContent` shrank by ~90 lines with zero behavior change (re-verified: menu bar "Settings…" still opens+raises correctly, log line `focusAfterOpening: raising window` fires same as before). Context (2) — ⌘, while Settings is already frontmost — needed **zero new code**: verified live (before touching anything) that SwiftUI's own auto-generated Settings-scene app-menu command already handles it once the app is `.regular` (which `SettingsActivator` already makes true for the duration Settings is open); confirmed via AX (`front window` name stays "General", `frontmost` stays true, no duplicate window — exactly 1 "General"-titled window before and after). Context (1) — picker open — hit a real, non-obvious bug during live verification, root-caused not guessed: adding a `KeyEquivalent(",")` case to `PickerView.handle(_:)`'s `.onKeyPress` compiled and looked correct but was dead code at runtime — `-[NSApplication sendEvent:]` matches a physical keyDown against `NSApp.mainMenu`'s key equivalents (the `Settings` scene auto-generates a hidden ⌘, item bound to its own `openSettings()`) *before* the key window's SwiftUI content ever sees it, even for an `.accessory`-policy app with no visible menu bar, as long as one of its own windows is `key` (the picker panel is). Reproduced with real evidence: with the naive `.onKeyPress` case in place, ⌘, while the picker was open silently created the Settings window via the built-in path (`CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo` showed it existed) but never raised/activated it (`onscreen=false`, `frontmostApplication` stayed whatever app was active, zero log output from my new code) — the exact T-SET1 failure mode recurring through a different door. Fixed the same way `onCommandDelete` already had to solve an analogous field-editor-preemption problem: intercepted ⌘, in `PickerPanel`'s existing local `NSEvent` monitor (which runs before AppKit's menu-key-equivalent matching), added `onCommandComma: (() -> Void)?`, renamed the backing token `commandDeleteMonitor` → `localKeyMonitor` (now serves two keys, doc comments/2 cross-references in `SettingsActivator.swift` updated to match) and restructured the handler into a `switch event.keyCode` on two cases; removed the now-genuinely-unreachable `.onKeyPress(",")` case from `PickerView` and documented why in both files. Wired `panel.onCommandComma = { viewModel?.openSettingsFromPicker() }` in `AppEnvironment.init()`, same shape as `onCommandDelete`. New `PickerViewModel.openSettingsFromPicker()` (`dismiss()` then `openSettings()`) — dismisses the picker first (a UX choice, not a technical necessity: the picker is a non-activating panel so leaving it open behind Settings wouldn't corrupt anything, but Settings takes over full app activation for as long as it's open, so a second Clipnest surface sitting open behind it would just be confusing); `openSettings: () -> Void` is an injected closure (same DI pattern as `dismiss`/`requestAppUpdate`) wired from `PickerView.onAppear` to `{ openSettings(); SettingsFocusCoordinator.focusAfterOpening() }` since `@Environment(\.openSettings)` is only reachable from a View. Footer hint: measured (`NSHostingView(rootView: Text(...).font(.caption2)).fittingSize`, same methodology as T-SET2/T-SET4) before adding — appending "· ⌘, settings" gives worst-case margins of +50pt (History text/link), +24pt (History OCR-image), +20pt (Snippets, the tightest) against the existing 493pt budget — all positive, added it as the final tab-independent hint; documented the shrunk-from-+85pt margin in `ShortcutHints.swift` so a future addition re-measures rather than assumes room. Fixed the misleading `ClipnestApp.swift` header comment the task flagged (claimed ⌘, worked when it never did) — now accurately describes all three reachable paths. Unit tests: `PickerViewModelOpenSettingsFromPickerTests` (2 new — dismiss-then-openSettings ordering via closure spies, matching the `dismiss`/`presentSnippetEditor` spy pattern already used in this file) + 1 new `ShortcutHintsTests` case (`⌘, settings` present in every tab/capability state) + updated 6 pre-existing exact-match hint-string assertions; explicitly NOT unit-tested (stated plainly, not silently skipped): the actual ⌘, key interception in `PickerPanel`'s `NSEvent` monitor and `SettingsFocusCoordinator`'s window-raising — AppKit/window-server-dependent, same class as `onCommandDelete`/`SettingsActivator`'s own established manual-only precedent. Manual verification on this machine (macOS 26.6.2), Accessibility tree (`front window`/`frontmost` via System Events) + `CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo` (screencapture TCC-blocked) + `log stream --predicate 'subsystem == "com.clipnest.app"' --level debug`, against a fresh `scripts/dev-install.sh` build so the very-first-ever-`openSettings()`-in-process case was genuinely exercised (not just steady-state): (1) picker open, ⌘, → picker panel `onscreen` flips true→false (dismissed), Settings window (`name=General`) flips false→true, `frontmostApplication` becomes `com.clipnest.app`, log shows `SettingsFocusCoordinator: focusAfterOpening: raising window` — confirmed on the very first press after a fresh launch (the harder, previously-broken timing case) and again on a repeat press. (2) Settings already focused, ⌘, again → `front window` name unchanged ("General"), `frontmost` stays true, exactly 1 "General" window before and after (no duplicate, no crash) — safe no-op via SwiftUI's own built-in handling, confirmed both via CGWindowList and independently via AX. - 2026-09-04 [T-UX1] Settings button to run OCR over existing images. Design: `ClipStore.fetchImagesNeedingRecognition()` (new protocol method, implemented in both `InMemoryClipStore`/`SwiftDataClipStore` — the store does the filtering via its existing predicate machinery, not the UI) returns every `.image` item with a blob but no recognized text, newest-first. New `Sources/ClipnestCore/OCR/OCRBackfillCoordinator.swift` (plain `Sendable` struct, Core, fully unit-tested — no actor isolation of its own) iterates that snapshot ONE item at a time through the injected `TextRecognizing`, writing via `ClipStore.setRecognizedText(_:text:)` — the exact same write path/recognizer `ClipboardMonitor` uses at capture time, so it inherits `VisionTextRecognizer.recognitionQueue`'s serial-queue T-HANG1 fix for free (no second execution path, no concurrency of its own). Deliberately decoupled from `SettingsStore.isTextRecognitionEnabled` per the user's own framing ("the user can choose when to run OCR") — `run(quality:onProgress:)` never reads or writes that setting, only the `quality` value passed in. Main-actor safety: the one genuinely-synchronous per-item step (the blob read) runs inside its own `Task.detached(priority: .utility)` (mirrors `ClipboardMonitor.checkNow()`'s identical pattern) so the coordinator is safe to call directly from `@MainActor` with no extra hop required at the call site; recognition itself was already off any Swift-concurrency thread (Vision's own dedicated queue). Cancellation: cooperative `Task.isCancelled` check between items only — never mid-item, so an in-flight item always finishes+writes before a cancellation takes effect (proved deterministically in tests via a gated fake recognizer + `task.cancel()` while item 1 is paused mid-flight, not a race on a progress signal — an earlier version of that test raced and intermittently cancelled after 2 items instead of 1, root-caused and fixed by switching from "wait for progress event" to "gate the recognizer call itself"). Resilience: missing/dangling blob, no text found, and an item deleted mid-run (`ClipStoreError.notFound` from `setRecognizedText`, same precedent as `scheduleTextRecognition`) all count as "completed, not recognized" and never abort the run — each proven with its own test. Idempotent by construction (work set only ever contains unrecognized images). App layer: `OCRBackfillViewModel` (`ClipnestApp/Sources/System/`, `@MainActor @Observable`) owns the one background `Task`, exposes `pendingCount`/`isRunning`/`progress`/`lastSummary`; `AppEnvironment` builds one shared `VisionTextRecognizer` instance now (hoisted into a local, was inline before) and passes it to BOTH `ClipboardMonitor` and the new coordinator, making the shared-serialization guarantee explicit rather than an accident of the queue being `static`. `HistorySettingsView` gained a new row: pending count ("N images have no recognized text" / "All images already have recognized text" when 0, never a no-op button), Run button, live "Recognizing… N of M" + Cancel while running, and a finished-state line (Done/Cancelled — recognized X of Y) — reuses whatever quality the existing Fast/Accurate picker is set to, doesn't duplicate any wording/logic from the T-OCR2 toggle above it. Manual-only (not unit-tested): the actual SwiftUI rendering/layout of `HistorySettingsView`'s new row (per coding-standards.md, `ClipnestApp` stays thin — light smoke tests only) — its data plumbing (`OCRBackfillViewModel`) IS covered by 3 smoke tests (full run reaching a finished state + `pendingCount` refresh, cancellation reaching a real in-flight run). Tests added: 4 new `ClipStoreContractTests` scenarios (wired ×2 for `InMemoryClipStore`/`SwiftDataClipStore` = 8 tests) covering work-set selection/no-blob exclusion/ordering/empty-when-done; 7 new `OCRBackfillCoordinatorTests` (happy path + exact progress sequence, empty work set, idempotency, per-item failure resilience, deleted-mid-run item, cancellation, `pendingCount`); 3 new `OCRBackfillViewModelTests` (App layer). Also added the missing `fetchImagesNeedingRecognition` stub to `ClipboardMonitorTests`'s pre-existing `ThrowingClipStore` fake (mechanical, required by the protocol addition, no behavior change). Left out: no separate count-only `ClipStore` query — `pendingCount()` re-fetches the same work-set query rather than adding a second, driftable predicate (cheap at this app's real scale, tens of images). No retroactive migration/backfill trigger on app launch — this is purely the user-initiated button the task asked for, nothing runs automatically. - 2026-09-04 [T-PF5c] One-shot migration of existing image rows' contentHash (byte-hash → decoded-pixel hash, D44/D45), built on already-landed `ImagePixelHashing`/`CoreGraphicsImagePixelHasher`/`OneShotStoreMigration`. `SwiftDataClipStore.init` gained a defaulted `imagePixelHasher: any ImagePixelHashing = CoreGraphicsImagePixelHasher()` param (mirrors `blobStore`'s injection shape); `AppEnvironment.swift`'s existing call site needs no change (confirmed, not edited). `prepare()` now runs a second, independently-gated `OneShotStoreMigration.run(...)` pass (own `imageContentHashBackfillCompleteDefaultsKeyPrefix`) after the existing `normalizedText` backfill: `backfillImageContentHashes(in:blobStore:hasher:)` fetches `.image` rows with `blobPath != nil`, reads each blob via `blobStore.read`, re-hashes via `hasher.pixelContentHash`, overwrites `record.contentHash`. Chosen failure semantics (stated explicitly per the task's ask): `BlobStoreError.notFound` (missing blob) and a `nil` hasher result (undecodable blob) are PERMANENT — logged metadata-only, row left unchanged, migration still reports success overall (retrying a blob that will never exist/decode is pointless and would block the marker forever). Any other `BlobStoreError.ioFailure` is treated as POTENTIALLY TRANSIENT — the whole migration reports `false` so the marker stays unset and the next `prepare()` (next launch) retries the full candidate set; rows already fixed before the transient failure are still saved (harmless to re-hash on retry). Actor-reentrancy note: `backfillImageContentHashes` is deliberately synchronous (not offloaded via `Task.detached` like `enforceRetention`'s blob-deletion fix) because `OneShotStoreMigration.run`'s `migration` param is a plain non-async `() -> Bool` closure (not touched, per spec) — no way to yield the actor mid-closure, and blocking-waiting on a detached Task from inside it would be a strictly worse anti-pattern (cooperative-pool thread exhaustion risk). Documented why this is safe today: `AppEnvironment.init` only assigns `clipStore`/`snippetStore` to `self` after `await`ing both stores' `prepare()` calls, so no other caller can be queued on this actor during the one-time scan. 6 new tests in `Tests/ClipnestCoreTests/SwiftDataClipStoreTests.swift`: legacy-hash row + real blob → backfilled to `CoreGraphicsImagePixelHasher`'s actual pixel hash; one-shot (call-counting `CountingImagePixelHasher` fake, on-disk store reopened twice, hasher invoked exactly once); missing-blob row left unchanged, no throw; non-image + blob-less-image rows untouched; undecodable-blob row left unchanged AND marker IS set (permanent-skip proof); transient `.ioFailure` (a directory placed at the blob path, so `fileExists` passes but `Data(contentsOf:)` throws) leaves the row unchanged AND marker NOT set on launch 1, then a second `prepare()` after fixing the transient condition successfully backfills it AND sets the marker (retry proof). `swift test --scratch-path .../pf5c-build`: "Test run with 282 tests in 20 suites passed" (0 failures, 0 regressions across the whole suite, not just this file). `swift format lint --recursive --strict Sources Tests` → exit 0. - 2026-09-05 [T-PERF3-CI] Fixed CI-only flake in checkNowClassifyDoesNotBlockTheMainActor (GH Actions run 33914926870, macos-26): verified NOT a production regression (25MB zero-filled fixture never reaches the new pixel hasher, falls to plain byte-hash SHA-256; `checkNow()`'s Task.detached offload confirmed intact via a temporarily-reverted-then-restored regression proof, `git diff` clean after). Root cause: `.serialized` only serializes within a suite, never across suites, so this session's new heavy-Task.detached suites (ImageContentHashBackfillCoordinatorTests, SwiftDataClipStoreTests backfill-under-load cases) can saturate the cooperative pool during T-PERF3's whole 5-attempt window on a small CI runner. First fix attempt (more attempts + real Task.sleep backoff) was PROVEN WRONG by instrumentation: on a heavily loaded machine it let a genuine reverted regression pass ~1/3 runs, because a real sleep's wake-up latency can distort one attempt's solo calibration relative to checkNow() moments later. Root-caused further: a SINGLE calibration sample is itself noise-prone (one attempt measured 0.157s vs siblings' 0.016-0.047s from a scheduling stall during just that call) and MORE attempts alone compounds this. Final fix: 5->10 attempts paced with Task.yield() (not sleep) + solo calibration hardened to min-of-5 samples (contention can only slow a call down, never speed it past its true cost, so min converges on ground truth without weakening detection). Verified both directions: regression proof (inline classify, no Task.detached) goes red every time in final testing (bestRatio 0.94, 10/10 clean catches in final config); 10+ consecutive clean full-suite runs (309/22, unchanged) across natural heavy ambient load (system load avg 30-50, unrelated processes), lighter load, and synthetic busy-loop load (20 procs/10 cores). - 2026-09-06 [T-LX1/T-LX2/T-LX3] Root cause of T-LX1 confirmed as reviewer's own independent finding: unowned `Thread { [weak self] in self?.receiveLoop() }` in `ClipnestControlService.start()` — `LinuxAppLifecycle.startControlService` held it as a purely local `let`, so ARC deallocated it (before the new pthread got scheduled) the instant that function returned; `receiveLoop()` never ran once, so the D-Bus control service claimed `app.clipnest.Clipnest` but could never answer a call. Fixed by giving it (and, per review escalation, the identically-shaped `StatusNotifierTray`/`ShellHelperClient`, plus `LinuxAppEnvironment` itself) an explicit static owner in `LinuxAppLifecycle` for the process lifetime. T-LX2: `ClipnestLogger`'s non-Apple branch was a true no-op for info/debug and printed errors to fully-buffered stdout — switched all 3 levels to a direct unbuffered `FileHandle.standardError` write; added rejection-reason logging (`ClipnestControlReceiveRejection`, pure + unit-tested) to `receiveLoop()`'s two real silent-`continue` paths. T-LX3: `clipnest-ctl` now bounds calls with `timeout --kill-after`, but ONLY when `existing_instance_running` (a `NameHasOwner` probe) confirms an instance already owns the bus — a first pass that blanket-bounded toggle-picker/expand-snippet was caught in independent review as a regression (it would kill a cold-started resident `clipnest` at the 15s mark), fixed and re-verified. Introspect's timeout was confirmed to be the same root cause, not a missing-Introspectable gap — no code change needed there. Runtime-verified end-to-end in the packaging/linux/vnc/Dockerfile image (Ping replies, toggle-picker/ping/open-settings complete in <200ms with no stray processes, and a cold-start instance survives past 18s untouched); macOS swift test 583/47 and Linux swift test 922 (918 baseline + 4 new) both green; swift-format lint byte-identical to pre-change baseline on both platforms (0 new warnings; 118 pre-existing warning lines in 3 untouched files left as-is). - 2026-09-06 [P8-C] Added UNIX fd passing to the hand-rolled D-Bus client so ShellHelperClient can move clipboard payloads. `DBusValue.unixFD(UInt32)` (type code `h`, alignment 4, marshals identically to `.uint32` — it's a wire INDEX, never the real fd); `DBusMessage` gained `unixFileDescriptorCount` + the `UNIX_FDS` header field (code 9), both defaulting to 0 so every pre-existing message is byte-identical. New `DBusFileDescriptorPassing.swift`: hand-computed `CMSG_ALIGN`/`CMSG_SPACE`/`CMSG_LEN`/`CMSG_DATA`/`CMSG_FIRSTHDR` (confirmed against glibc's real `bits/socket.h` inside `swift:6.0-jammy`, not guessed) wrapping `sendmsg`/`recvmsg` — these two ARE directly callable from Swift (verified; unlike `ioctl`/D59, neither is C-variadic, so no dlopen/dlsym needed). `DBusConnection` now has fd-aware `send`/`call`/`receiveOneMessageWithFileDescriptors`; received fds are FIFO-claimed off a `pendingFileDescriptors` queue by each decoded message's own `unixFileDescriptorCount` (correct for this app's strictly-sequential per-connection usage, documented why), and any fd a non-fd-aware caller didn't ask for is defensively closed rather than leaked (`deinit` sweeps leftovers too). Root-caused via the diagnosing-bugs loop why the mechanism silently dropped fds against a REAL daemon despite correct marshalling: the SASL handshake never sent `NEGOTIATE_UNIX_FD`/checked `AGREE_UNIX_FD` (D-Bus spec requires this before ANY fd travels on a connection) — fixed in `DBusAuthHandshake`/`DBusConnection.performExternalAuth()`. Wired the 5 payload members into `ShellHelperRequests`/`ShellHelperResponses`/`ShellHelperClient` (`SetClipboardWatch`, `GetClipboardMimeTypes`, `ReadClipboard`, `SetClipboard`, `ClipboardChanged`); added `ShellHelperClipboardSelection` (verified against Mutter's real `MetaSelectionType` header via deepwiki, not guessed: PRIMARY=1, CLIPBOARD=3). Extended `DBusCalling` with a defaulted fd-aware `call` overload (non-breaking — `FakeDBusCalling` needs no change). 31 new unit tests (marshalling, a real `socketpair`+`SCM_RIGHTS` round trip — no daemon needed so it's real automated CI coverage not just manual-verify, protocol builders/parsers, `ShellHelperClient` wiring incl. real-pipe-fd leak verification via `fcntl(F_GETFD)`). Proved the full mechanism end-to-end against a REAL dbus-daemon: built `clipnest-p8c` from `packaging/linux/vnc/Dockerfile`, read the running app's actual `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS` from `/proc/<pid>/environ` (avoided the double-bus trap), ran a two-peer harness compiled from byte-identical copies of the real production D-Bus source files (`-static-stdlib`, no SPM target added) — a real file's bytes survived `sendmsg`+`SCM_RIGHTS` through the daemon and back exactly (pasted output in return message). macOS `swift test` 583/47 unaffected (Linux targets are `#if os(Linux)`-gated, confirmed by running it). Linux `swift test` on the full current integrated tree (my changes + P8-A + P8-B, all still uncommitted) 979/979 green; my own touched/new files lint-clean (0 warnings). Necessary touches beyond the literal file list, judged in-scope and noted for the spawner: `DBusCalling.swift` (additive protocol requirement + default impl — required for ShellHelperClient's fd calls to route through the existing `any DBusCalling` seam) and `ShellHelperProtocol.swift`/`ShellHelperNames.swift` (the 5 request/response builders that file's own doc comment explicitly flagged as this task's follow-up). → status:test, returning to spawner (rule 1); decisions below are for whoever holds project-context.md's pen.

- 2026-09-06 [P10-D] Replaced the Linux picker's 33ms `PickerWindow+Polling.swift` GLib timeout with real push notification. `ClipnestObservation/ObservableObject.swift`: `ObservableObjectPublisher` is now a real pub/sub (`subscribe(_:) -> ObservationCancellable`, `send()` invokes every registered handler; `ObservationCancellable.cancel()`/`deinit` unregisters, mirroring `Combine.AnyCancellable`'s auto-cancel-on-deinit) — fixes both original defects (`send()` was a no-op; the default `objectWillChange` handed back a fresh instance per access). Fixed defect 2 by REMOVING the generic default entirely (no `ObjectIdentifier`-keyed side table — a leak-by-design the task ruled out) and requiring each conformer to store its own instance: `PickerViewModel.swift` gets ONE new stored `public let objectWillChange = ObservableObjectPublisher()` under `#if !canImport(Combine)`, replicating by hand what the Swift compiler auto-synthesizes for real `Combine.ObservableObject` conformers. Concurrency, decided deliberately: `ObservableObjectPublisher`/`ObservationCancellable` stay plain `nonisolated` (`@unchecked Sendable`, upgraded from the old stateless `Sendable`) rather than `@MainActor`-isolated — empirically verified (`swiftc -strict-concurrency=complete`) that an `@MainActor` version fails to compile at `Published`'s generic `_enclosingInstance` subscript call site (itself necessarily `nonisolated`, since it's generic over `EnclosingSelf: ObservableObject` with no actor constraint); documented invariant instead, matching this codebase's own `BlobStore`/`PickerWindow` "single thread by construction" precedent (`PickerViewModel`, the sole conformer, is `@MainActor`; `PickerWindow`, the sole subscriber, already proves GTK-thread==MainActor-executor-thread). Coalescing: new `PickerWindowRefreshCoalescer` (zero GTK dependency, fully unit-tested) tracks one boolean; `PickerWindow+Reconcile.swift` (renamed from `+Polling.swift`) schedules at most one `g_idle_add_full` per batch of notifications, re-arming only once that idle callback actually starts — proven via a real unit test that 5 notifications in one turn produce exactly 1 scheduled refresh. `show()` now subscribes BEFORE calling `willShow()` (which itself synchronously fires 4 `@Published` mutations) then renders one synchronous first frame; `hide()` cancels the subscription + any pending idle source. Audited every mutation path per the task's requirement 5: `handleNewCapture`/OCR-completion/retention-triggered requeries/pin-toggle/delete all assign `@Published` properties normally (traced through `PagedQuery`'s `setItems`/`appendItems` closures) — found ONE pre-existing gap, NOT a regression and NOT touched (out of scope, identical on macOS `AppEnvironment`): `scheduleRetentionEnforcement()` (both `LinuxAppEnvironment` and macOS `AppEnvironment`) never re-queries `PickerViewModel` after `enforceRetention`, so a retention-trimmed row can stay visible until the next unrelated requery — pre-existing under the OLD poll too (poll only diffed `viewModel.rows`, never re-read the store), just newly visible by inspection since I read every mutation path end-to-end. Also found (via `LinuxAppEnvironment.showPicker`, another agent's file, not touched): it calls `pickerViewModel.willShow()` explicitly BEFORE `pickerWindow.show(at:)` (which calls `willShow()` again internally) — a harmless pre-existing double-call (the second, observed call's query supersedes the first's per `PagedQuery`'s cancel-before-start), unrelated to and unchanged by this task. `PickerWindow+Rows.swift`: added a targeted (not general-diff) optimization for requirement 6 — `updateRows`/`updateSnippetRows` take an incremental append path (unit-tested `appendedSuffixStart(old:new:)`) only when `rows` is an exact-prefix growth (pagination's real, existing `rows.append(contentsOf:)` shape) AND neither tab nor search text changed since the last reconcile; every other shape (search replacing the list, a capture PREPENDING an item, a pin/unpin re-sort) falls back to the existing, unchanged full rebuild — general keyed diffing deliberately not attempted (documented why: real widget-reuse/selection-preservation edges, a separate task). REAL BUG found + fixed via this task's own mandated runtime verification (not by inspection): `clearListBox()`'s `while let child = gtk_widget_get_first_child(listBox) { remove }` infinite-loops the instant a rebuild needs to clear the LAST real row, because `previewPopover` is a genuine permanent widget-tree child of `listBox` (`gtk_widget_set_parent(previewPopover, listBox)` in `PickerWindow+Layout.swift`, pre-existing, not mine) that `gtk_list_box_remove` correctly refuses ("Tried to remove non-child") forever once exposed — pre-existing latent bug, newly triggered by my own correct "close and reopen the picker with exactly one history item" reconcile path; produced a hung GTK main thread and a 2.4GB/58M-line log in the wild before I isolated and fixed it. Fix: stop the loop at `child == previewPopover` (verified empirically: real rows always precede the popover in child order) plus a defensive 1000-iteration cap as a backstop. Chose a wrapper over adding this to the `ClipStore` protocol itself specifically because a protocol requirement would force `SwiftDataClipStore`/`SQLiteClipStore` (both outside my file ownership for this task) to each thread a `changes.send(...)` through their own mutating methods by hand — exactly the "next call site has to remember" failure mode the task ruled out, just moved one layer down; a decorator keeps the broadcasting logic in exactly one place and every consumer gets it for free by construction, since this codebase already injects `any ClipStore` everywhere from one composition root. Rejected Combine (doesn't exist on Linux; `ClipStore` must compile identically on both platforms with no `#if canImport(Combine)`) and `ClipnestObservation.ObservableObjectPublisher` (P10-D's real pub/sub, but scoped to Combine/`@Published` mimicry, payload-less, and `ClipnestCore` has no dependency on that target — would be a backwards dependency edge) — full reasoning in `NotifyingClipStore`'s doc comment. `AppEnvironment`/`LinuxAppEnvironment` each wrap their real store immediately after construction (`let clipStore = NotifyingClipStore(wrapping: rawClipStore)`) and pass THIS value to every consumer (`clipboardMonitor`, `pickerViewModel`, `ocrBackfillViewModel`, `HistorySettingsView`/`SettingsWindow`'s injected `clipStore`) — so `PickerViewModel.delete(_:)`/`togglePin(_:)`, Settings' clear-history, and retention enforcement all broadcast automatically with zero per-call-site opt-in. Both composition roots subscribe once to forward `.deleted`/`.clearedAll` into `ClipboardMonitor`'s existing (T-HANG5) `cancelPendingRecognition(for:)`/`cancelAllPendingRecognition()` (closing T-HANG6 without `PickerViewModel` ever needing a `ClipboardMonitor` reference). `PickerViewModel` takes a new optional `storeChanges:` init param (default `nil`, fully backward-compatible) and subscribes to react to `.clearedAll`/`.retentionApplied` only (re-querying the Rows pipeline via the existing `scheduleRowsQuery`) — deliberately ignores `.inserted`/`.updated`/`.deleted` since those are already handled by `onCapture`/its own mutation methods; a dedicated test proves no double-handling. Hit and fixed a real Swift definite-initialization bug along the way: capturing `[weak self]` in the subscription closure partway through `AppEnvironment`/`LinuxAppEnvironment`'s `init` failed to compile ("variable 'self.X' used before being initialized") since Swift requires every stored property initialized before the FIRST self-escaping closure in source order — fixed by capturing `[weak monitor]` (already in scope) instead of `self`, and by making the new `clipStoreChangeSubscription` property `var` with an implicit-`nil` Optional default (matching this file's own existing `pendingRetentionTask` pattern) rather than `let`. `SettingsWindow+History.swift` (Linux, GTK): unchanged — at the isolated build baseline (HEAD `ba9fd74`) this file has no "Clear All History" feature yet (it's being added by a concurrent, uncommitted parallel-agent diff not in my file ownership); once that lands calling `clipStore.clearHistory()` through the composition-root-injected (now-wrapped) `clipStore`, T-RT2 is fixed with no further change to that file. - 2026-09-06 [T-OPT2] Settings > Shortcuts was a fully read-only reference list on Linux and never showed the global toggle-picker hotkey at all. Built the global-shortcut section: `GlobalHotkeyRecorder` (in `SettingsWindow+Shortcuts.swift`, never stored on `SettingsWindow` — a Swift extension can't add stored properties, and it lives entirely as `gtkConnect`'s own retained `user_data` for the two connections it's created for, matching this window's permanent-for-app-lifetime contract) drives a "Record New Shortcut…" flow: a `GtkEventControllerKey` attached to the window (`GTK_PHASE_CAPTURE`, mirroring `PickerWindow+Keyboard.swift`'s identical reasoning) captures the next key press, `Support/GlobalHotkeyAcceleratorValidation.swift` (new, pure, reuses `KeyEventMapping`'s modifier masks) validates it and converts to GTK's own canonical `gtk_accelerator_name` string form, `Hotkeys/GlobalHotkeyAccelerator.swift` (new) persists it via `g_settings_set_strv` directly to the shared `app.clipnest.Clipnest.Keybindings` schema's `toggle-picker` key — the SAME schema the GNOME Shell extension reads, so no IPC is needed — and `ClipnestLinuxAppKit/Hotkeys/ToggleHotkeyFloorBinding.swift` (new) re-installs the GSettings custom-keybinding floor (`GSettingsCustomKeybinding.install`, unchanged) with the new accelerator so it also works without the extension. Real finding, NOT assumed: verified empirically via a throwaway `swift:6.0-jammy`+`libgtk-4-dev` probe package that `gtk_accelerator_valid` alone treats a BARE unmodified printable key (plain `v`, no modifier) as a valid accelerator — correct for a menu, wrong for a global hotkey — so `GlobalHotkeyAcceleratorValidation` adds its own "at least one of Control/Alt/Shift/Super" requirement on top (the task's "reject an unmodified accelerator"); `gtk_accelerator_valid` itself does still correctly reject a bare modifier keypress alone, even while ANOTHER modifier is already held (verified: Shift_L pressed while Control is down is rejected), so that's reused rather than reimplemented. `GdkModifierType(rawValue:)` and the whole accelerator-utility family (`gtk_accelerator_valid/_name/_parse/_get_label`) work with no display/`gtk_init()` at all — confirmed via the same probe — so `GlobalHotkeyAcceleratorValidation`'s tests run headless in CI. Schema-absence is handled per D68 (`schemaIsInstalled()` guards every `g_settings_new*` call — a missing schema ABORTS the process, `guard let` provides zero protection) — verified live in the swift:6.0-jammy dev container (no `.deb` ever installed there, so the schema genuinely is absent) that `current()`/`write(_:)` fail safe rather than crash. Schema ID/key name are a small, deliberately duplicated copy of `ShellExtensionKeybindingSchema`'s (`ClipnestLinuxAppKit/DBus/ShellHelperNames.swift`) — `ClipnestGTK` cannot import that module (`Package.swift`'s dependency edge runs the other way) — documented inline, matching `LinuxShortcutDescriptions.swift`'s own established precedent for the identical cross-module-boundary shape. Same reasoning for `ToggleHotkeyFloorBinding.segment`/`.bindingLabel` vs. `LinuxAppLifecycle`'s own (`private`, unreachable) copy of the identical literals — pinned by a new regression test (`AppToggleHotkeyFloorBindingTests`) asserting today's literal value against `GSettingsKeybindingPath`, plus a recommended follow-up (not applied — `LinuxAppLifecycle.swift` wasn't in this task's owned-files scope) to hoist both into one shared constant. Reused existing UI primitives throughout (`addErrorLabel`/`addStatusLabel`/`setStatusLabel`'s exact two-line body, small documented duplicate since `GlobalHotkeyRecorder` isn't an extension of `SettingsWindow`) rather than inventing new ones. **Cross-module wiring this task cannot make itself** (`SettingsWindow.swift`/`LinuxAppEnvironment.swift` are owned by other concurrent agents/the spawner): `SettingsWindow` needs one new closure, `let reinstallToggleHotkeyFloor: (_ accelerator: String) -> Void`, threaded through its `init` exactly like `launchAtLoginProvider`/`setLaunchAtLogin` already are; `LinuxAppEnvironment`'s `SettingsWindow(...)` call site needs `reinstallToggleHotkeyFloor: { accelerator in ToggleHotkeyFloorBinding.reinstallFloor(withAccelerator: accelerator) }` added alongside its existing arguments. Verified this exact shape compiles and works end-to-end by applying it ONLY to an isolated rsync'd scratch copy of the working tree (never the shared real files) — real files are untouched, this wiring is reported here for the pen-holder to apply. Verification: Linux (`swift:6.0-jammy` + `libgtk-4-dev`, isolated scratch copy with the wiring stub applied) `swift build` clean; `swift test` → **1068/1068 passed** (16 new: 11 `GlobalHotkeyAcceleratorValidationTests`, 2 `GlobalHotkeyAcceleratorTests`, 3 `ToggleHotkeyFloorBindingTests`); `swift format lint --strict` clean on all 8 touched/created files. macOS `swift test` → **605 tests, 51 suites, exact baseline** (every touched file is Linux-only, `#if os(Linux)` in `Package.swift`, never enters the macOS graph). Full runtime proof via the official `packaging/linux/vnc/Dockerfile` (real `dpkg-buildpackage`+`.deb` install, D-Bus address read from `/proc/<pid>/environ`, `xdotool windowactivate --sync` + plain `xdotool key` per this task's documented traps): screenshot shows "Open Clipnest (global shortcut) — Alt+Super+V" (the schema's real default, not hardcoded) + "Record New Shortcut…"; clicking it + `xdotool key --clearmodifiers ctrl+alt+k` updates the label to "Ctrl+Alt+K", `gsettings get app.clipnest.Clipnest.Keybindings toggle-picker` confirms `['<Control><Alt>k']` persisted; an unmodified `v` press shows the inline red error "A global shortcut needs at least one modifier key..." and STAYS in recording mode; Escape cancels cleanly. Found + fixed 2 real bugs via this runtime pass, not by inspection: (1) `cancelRecording()` cleared the status label but left a rejected attempt's error message visibly stuck after Escape — now clears both; (2) neither `addErrorLabel`/`addStatusLabel` wrap text, and this tab's messages are long enough that the whole 480px Settings window stretched to >1000px to fit one unwrapped line — fixed by calling `gtk_label_set_wrap`/`gtk_label_set_max_width_chars` on just these two labels (not the shared builder, out of scope).
