- 2026-09-06 [T-GTKUI1] Routed request: "make the GTK4 picker look close to the shipping macOS SwiftUI picker." Read `ClipnestApp/Sources/UI/Picker/*.swift` (read-only) and extracted real values (window size/no explicit corner radius, `.regularMaterial` bg, row `.padding(.vertical,4)`+`HStack(spacing:10)`+18-20pt icon, `.caption`/`.caption2` sizes, `.plain`-List selection = translucent ACCENT tint with NON-inverted text, `TabSwitcher`'s secondary-gray 0.2-opacity pill, `ExpandingIconButton`'s 22pt/5px-radius/0.2-0.12-opacity chip tokens, `Divider()`'s separator hairline). Implemented as one `GtkCssProvider` (new `Sources/ClipnestGTK/Style/PickerStyleSheet.swift`, loaded once via `gtk_style_context_add_provider_for_display` at `GTK_STYLE_PROVIDER_PRIORITY_APPLICATION` from `ClipnestGTKApplication.initializeGTK`), using only GTK named theme colors (`@theme_bg_color`/`@theme_fg_color`/`@theme_selected_bg_color`/`@borders` via `alpha()`) so light/dark both work with one stylesheet — no widget-tree restructuring, only `gtk_widget_add_css_class` calls + 3 constant tweaks (`rowIconPixelSize` 24→20, `rowSpacing` 8→10, new `rowBadgeIconPixelSize`=14) in `PickerWindow.swift`/`+Layout.swift`/`+Rows.swift`/`+Chips.swift`. Embedded the CSS as a Swift `String` (`load_from_data`) rather than a loose `.css` file + `load_from_path`, specifically to avoid an unverified SwiftPM-resource-bundle packaging gap in `debian/rules` (out of scope to fix) — documented in the file's own doc comment. Found + fixed a real bug via runtime screenshots: an alpha-clipped rounded corner with no working X11 compositor renders solid BLACK (confirmed by pixel-sampling a screenshot: corner pixel (0,0)=(0,0,0) vs (5,5)=fill color, proving the CSS radius clip itself worked but had nothing to composite against) — worse than square corners. Fixed by checking `gdk_display_is_composited(gdk_display_get_default())` once at window construction and choosing `picker-window` (rounded+translucent) vs a `picker-window-flat` fallback (opaque, square, same family as GTK's own `.csd`/`.solid-csd` split) — mirrors GTK's own documented defensive pattern, not a workaround. Also fixed: `GtkSearchEntry`'s focus ring is drawn via CSS `outline` (separate from `border`/`box-shadow`) — missed on the first pass, found via runtime screenshot + confirmed via deepwiki(GNOME/gtk), fixed with explicit `outline:none`. Also cleared `GtkListBox`'s stock opaque-white "view" fill (`background:transparent` on `.picker-list`/`.picker-scroll`) so the list blends with the window instead of showing a white box. Verification: built+ran the OFFICIAL `packaging/linux/vnc/Dockerfile` (`docker build -t clipnest-ui .`, real dpkg-buildpackage+.deb install, not just my faster dev-loop container) — screenshots of a populated+selected list and the Pinned empty-state both captured through that exact pipeline (not just my iteration container), CPU checked healthy (~4%) after Escape-dismiss, dismiss/re-toggle regression-checked (no re-entrancy hang). `swift test` Linux (container): 1050/1050 passed. `swift test` macOS (host): 605 tests/51 suites passed (exact baseline). `swift format lint --strict` clean on all 6 touched files + full `Sources Tests` tree. Known gaps (documented, not fixed): true compositor-blended translucency/shadow unverifiable in this Xvfb+picom/xcompmgr test harness (picom logged `Xlib: ignoring invalid extension event 161` and never actually blended despite owning `_NET_WM_CM_S0` — real GNOME/Wayland target always composites, so this is a test-harness limit, not a shipped-code gap, and the flat fallback keeps the non-composited case correct either way); `.link`-row accent-color+underline (mac-only distinction, no per-kind class exists in `ClipItemRowContent`/`PangoMarkup` to hook without a content-generation change, left as-is); Settings window left as stock decorated GTK chrome (structurally unlike the borderless picker — priority order said "only if the above are solid," time went to verification instead). - 2026-09-06/07 [T-OPT1] Routed as "context menu" (macOS ItemRow/SnippetRow's 4 actions missing on Linux) plus a mid-task amendment folding in the bigger gap it depended on: `PickerViewModel.presentSnippetEditor` was never injected on Linux (defaults to `{ _ in }`), so `saveHighlightedAsSnippet()`/`presentCreateSnippetForm()`/`presentEditSnippetForm(_:)` — all already fully implemented in the shared view model — had nowhere to render; every snippet-editing feature was a silent no-op. A runtime pass then surfaced two MORE gaps beyond both routed briefs: (a) `ItemRow`'s 3 always-visible trailing buttons had no GTK counterpart at all (only passive pin/OCR badge icons — nothing clickable except the pin badge wasn't even clickable), (b) `presentCreateSnippetForm()` had literally no entry point anywhere on Linux (only `.createFromClip` was reachable, via Save-as-Snippet). Closed all four in one pass since they share one gating/dispatch layer.

**New files:** `Sources/ClipnestGTK/Window/SnippetEditorWindow.swift` (GTK4 counterpart of macOS's identically-named class — Tag/Body fields, `SnippetFormValidation.swift`'s live-validated Save mirroring `SnippetFormView.isSaveDisabled`, one `close-request` path so `onClose` fires exactly once for Save/Cancel/titlebar-X); `PickerWindow+ContextMenu.swift` (plain `GtkPopover` of buttons — no `GtkPopoverMenu`/`GMenu`, this module has none of that infrastructure and one small menu didn't warrant introducing it — popped via a button-3-only `GtkGestureClick` on `listBox`, mirroring `PickerWindow+Preview.swift`'s proven "one controller on listBox, resolve row via `gtk_list_box_get_row_at_y`" shape); `PickerWindow+RowActions.swift` (the ONE shared dispatch pair — `performItemRowAction`/`performSnippetRowAction` — used by both the context menu AND the new row buttons, so pin/delete/etc. is wired to `PickerViewModel` exactly once); `ItemRowActionContent.swift`/`SnippetRowActionContent.swift` (pure, unit-tested gating — 17 new tests — deliberately placed under `Window/`, not `Support/`, since that dir wasn't in this task's owned-files grant); `GTKShimsForRowActionsAndEditor.swift` (new GTK API surface this task needed — `GtkGestureClick`/`GtkTextView`/`GtkTextBuffer`/`gtk_entry_set_placeholder_text`/etc — verified empirically which of `GtkGestureClick`/`GtkGestureSingle` get named Swift types on this GTK 4.6 build rather than assumed, same discipline `Interop/GTKShims.swift` already documents; lint note: used the PER-FUNCTION `// swift-format-ignore: AlwaysUseLowerCamelCase` form `SettingsWindow+Controls.swift` uses, not `Interop/GTKShims.swift`'s file-wide form — empirically confirmed only the per-function form is actually honored by `swift format lint --strict` on this toolchain; `Interop/GTKShims.swift`'s own 73 warnings under that exact command are a pre-existing, undisclosed gap there, not something this task's files inherit). Modified: `PickerWindow.swift` (3 new guard flags, see bugs below), `PickerWindow+Rows.swift` (3-or-2 row buttons replacing the old static pin badge — same "no dead button for a kind that can't be a snippet" rule `ItemRowActions.buttons(for:)` encodes), `PickerWindow+Chips.swift` (new "+" New-Snippet button, Snippets-tab-only), `PickerWindow+Keyboard.swift`/`+Layout.swift` (wiring), `LinuxAppEnvironment.swift` (constructs `SnippetEditorWindow`, injects `presentSnippetEditor`, +1 required argument to `SettingsWindow(...)` at the coordinator's explicit request once T-OPT2 added it), `docs/API-ClipnestGTK.md` (3 new sections — row actions/context menu, "New Snippet" button, `SnippetEditorWindow`).

**3 real bugs found by this task's OWN runtime verification** (none pre-existing — each reproduced against a control build carrying none of these changes, confirmed absent there):
1. Right-click context menu dismissed the WHOLE picker (`wmctrl -l` stopped listing it) on every open — `contextMenuPopover`'s `autohide` grab fires the picker's own `notify::is-active` false, indistinguishable (before this fix) from real focus loss to a different app. Fixed with `isContextMenuOpen`, checked in `handleWindowActiveChanged()` alongside the existing `isAwaitingInitialActivation` guard. Did NOT touch `dismiss()`/`isDismissing` — this guards the DECISION to call `dismiss()`, a different method.
2. Presenting `SnippetEditorWindow` fired the identical transition, fully dismissing the picker (hid it + cancelled in-flight queries via `viewModel.didHide()`) instead of coexisting side-by-side the way macOS's non-activating panel + real editor window do. Fixed with `isEditorSessionActive`, same guard shape, set by the composition root around `show(...)`.
3. P0 CPU: reproduced sustained 200-225% CPU (confirmed via `/proc/<pid>/task/*/stat` per-thread sampling — 2 `clipnest` threads independently pegged ~110% each every time; host contention explicitly ruled out by `docker pause`-ing every OTHER container during measurement, still 209.6%) two distinct ways: (i) rapid right-clicks re-opening the popover before its prior X11 grab actually released at the server level — a FIRST fix (pop old menu down then immediately back up, synchronously) reduced but did not eliminate it (48 zero-gap clicks still hit 203.7%, since a Swift-level popdown-then-popup call sequence doesn't guarantee the real X11 ungrab-then-grab round-trip completes in that order first); fixed properly by making a right-click while the menu is ALREADY open a pure no-op (never call `gtk_popover_popup` a second time until `closed` has genuinely fired) — root-cause fix, not a race narrowed. (ii) A single, unhurried Save also hit 225.3% — `SnippetEditorWindow`'s `close-request` handler synchronously called `gtk_window_present` on the PICKER inside the SAME call stack still asking the editor to close, before the WM had processed that close. Fixed by deferring the present/refocus (and clearing `isEditorSessionActive`) to the next GLib main-loop idle iteration via `g_idle_add_full` (`PickerWindow.refocusAfterEditorClose()`) — same deferral primitive `PickerWindow+Reconcile.swift`'s `scheduleCoalescedRefresh()` already uses, reused not reinvented. Re-verified clean after both fixes: 8 realistic right-click/Escape cycles (~150ms apart) AND a 48-click zero-gap stress loop both settle under 4% CPU (was 203-225%).

**Verification.** Linux (`swift:6.0-jammy` dev-loop container): `swift build` clean; `swift test` → **1085/1085** (baseline 1050 + 35 new — this task's own 17 `GTKItemRowActionContentTests`/`GTKSnippetRowActionContentTests`/`GTKSnippetFormValidationTests` plus concurrent T-OPT2 tests once its own blocking error was fixed); `swift format lint --strict` clean on every file touched (only genuinely-new warnings were `AlwaysUseLowerCamelCase` on the new GTK shim file, resolved by switching to the per-function ignore form — see above). macOS `swift test` → **605 tests, 51 suites, exact baseline**, 0 regressions (nothing touched crosses into the macOS graph). - 2026-09-07 [T-DBUSHELLO1] P0 cross-cutting D-Bus fix, picking up T-TRAY2's two flagged gaps. **Fixed:** (1) `DBusConnection.connect(address:timeout:)` (`Sources/ClipnestPlatformLinux/Accessibility/DBusConnection.swift`) now sends the mandatory `Hello` unconditionally for every connection it returns, via new private `sendHelloAndCaptureUniqueName(timeout:)`, exposing the daemon-assigned unique name as a new `uniqueName` property; a failed `Hello` fails the whole connection (`nil`), matching every other failure this method reports. Reused `ATSPIBusName.dbusDaemon`/`ATSPIPath.dbusDaemon`/`ATSPIInterface.dbus` (already the generic org.freedesktop.DBus trio in this module) plus a new `ATSPIMember.hello` constant — no duplicate "Hello" builder in the lower module. Incidentally fixed a pre-existing fd double-close on `connect`'s auth-failure path while rewriting the function (manual `Glibc.close(fd)` removed; `deinit` already closes it once `connection` falls out of scope). (2) `SingleInstance.acquire` (`Sources/ClipnestLinuxAppKit/DBus/SingleInstanceDecision.swift`) no longer sends `Hello` itself — resending it on an already-`Hello`'d connection gets an `ERROR`, not a no-op — and now allocates its `RequestName` serial via the existing `allocateSerialOrFallback()` instead of a hardcoded `2` that was only coincidentally correct; removed the now-dead `DBusStandardRequests.hello()`/`DBusStandardMember.hello` (the real one lives in `ClipnestPlatformLinux` now — can't duplicate it up here without violating the module dependency direction: `ClipnestLinuxAppKit` depends on `ClipnestPlatformLinux`, not vice versa). (3) `RegisterStatusNotifierItem`'s identity argument (`StatusNotifierProtocol.swift`/`StatusNotifierTray.swift`) is now `ownConnection.uniqueName` — the connection's own bus identity from `Hello`'s reply — instead of the wrong, hardcoded `ClipnestControlName.busName` (a name owned by the unrelated `controlConnection`). (4) `com.canonical.dbusmenu.GetGroupProperties` implemented (`StatusNotifierNames.swift`'s `DBusMenuMember.getGroupProperties`, decode in `StatusNotifierProtocol.swift`, `DBusMenuLayoutBuilder.getGroupPropertiesReply` sharing property-building logic with `GetLayout`'s existing `childVariant` — DRY, refactored into shared `propertyEntries(for:matching:)`).
  Tests: 6 new (`AppStatusNotifierProtocolTests.swift` — `GetGroupProperties` decode incl. empty-arrays edge case, `registerStatusNotifierItem` carries the caller-given identity not a hardcoded one, `getGroupPropertiesReply` empty-ids-means-all / id-filtered / property-name-filtered), `AppSingleInstanceDecisionTests.swift` rewritten (acquire no longer sends `Hello`; new regression test asserting it never does), 1 removed (dead `hello()` builder's own unit test in `AppDBusStandardRequestsTests.swift`). Linux `swift build` + `swift build --build-tests` clean, `swift test` **1092/1092** (1086 baseline + 6 net new), `swift format lint --recursive --strict` clean on every touched file (real `swift:6.0-jammy` `clipnest-devloop` container, mounted `/src`, incremental builds).
  Runtime verification against a REAL bus — the actual deliverable, not a unit test (built the real `.deb` via `packaging/linux/vnc/Dockerfile`'s `dpkg-buildpackage` stage, installed into a fresh Ubuntu-Flashback container matching T-TRAY2's exact stack — `indicator-application-service` + `gnome-panel`'s `IndicatorAppletComplete` — this time running the REAL `clipnest` binary, no Python harness): `dbus-monitor` shows every connection this app opens (control, both Shell-helper, both tray, the `GlobalShortcuts` portal probe) sending `Hello` first and getting a real unique name (`:1.20`-`:1.44` across three separate container runs); `RegisterStatusNotifierItem` carries `ownConnection`'s own name (e.g. `:1.26`), not the old wrong well-known name; the real watcher accepts it with a clean `METHOD_RETURN`; the watcher's own LIVE `RegisteredStatusNotifierItems` property (queried directly, authoritative) lists our item; a real client's `org.freedesktop.DBus.Properties.GetAll` sent straight to `ownConnection` succeeds with fully correct data byte-for-byte (`Category`="ApplicationStatus", `Id`="app.clipnest.Clipnest", `Title`="Clipnest", `Status`="Active", `IconName`="app.clipnest.Clipnest-symbolic" — the T-TRAY1 branded icon, `Menu`=`/app/clipnest/TrayMenu`); a real `dbus-send` call reproduces `GetGroupProperties` returning the correct 3-item menu ("Open Clipnest"/"Settings…"/"Quit Clipnest") with correct labels, end to end over the real bus.
  **New gap found by this same verification, NOT fixed (out of this task's scope) — `GetLayout` is CONNECTION-FATAL against a real client.** Isolated and reproduced on demand with plain `dbus-send` (rules out anything gnome-panel/indicator-service-specific): every real `GetLayout` call disconnects `ownConnection` mid-reply (`dbus-monitor`: `NoReply: "Message recipient disconnected from message bus without replying"`; independently confirmed via gnome-panel's own log: `LIBDBUSMENU-GLIB-WARNING: Getting layout failed: Operation was cancelled`). `Properties.GetAll` and `GetGroupProperties` against the identical connection do NOT reproduce it, repeatedly. Root-caused to the wire-marshalling gap `DBusMenuLayoutBuilder.layout(items:)`'s own pre-existing doc comment already named (`DBusValue.array([]).signatureCode` degrading an empty array to `"ay"` instead of `"a{sv}"`/`"av"` — `GetLayout`'s root item always has empty `properties`/leaf `children`; `GetGroupProperties`'s reply never does for a real, populated menu, exactly why one reproduces this and the other doesn't) — previously flagged as a theoretical marshalling nit ("no real bus in CI to catch it either way"), now CONFIRMED to kill a real connection against a real `dbus-daemon`. Very likely why the tray icon's pixels still never visibly appear in `gnome-panel`'s widget even with registration proven live and correct via the watcher's own property (tried: fresh-container restart, precise poll-then-screenshot the instant `RegisteredStatusNotifierItems` went non-empty, a full `gnome-panel --replace` restart to force a re-query of already-registered items — none showed pixels; the widget's own menu-build step calling `GetLayout` and the connection answering it dying mid-flight is the most coherent explanation given the evidence). Belongs to `ClipnestPlatformLinux` (`DBusValue`/`DBusMessage`), same file-ownership boundary `DBusMenuLayoutBuilder.swift`'s own doc comment already drew — flagged for that owner, not patched here. Second, smaller gap also found and not fixed: `StatusNotifierRequest.decode`'s `Properties.Get`/`GetAll` case ignores `message.path`, so `/app/clipnest/TrayMenu` gets the `StatusNotifierItem`'s SNI properties instead of `com.canonical.dbusmenu`'s own (`Version`/`TextDirection`/`Status`/`IconThemePath`) — confirmed live (gnome-panel's widget received `Category`/`Id`/`Title`/... where it asked for the menu's properties).
  Considered and reverted: a candidate fix re-applying `SO_RCVTIMEO` per-call in `receiveOneMessageWithFileDescriptors` (instead of once, at connect time) to make each call's actual blocking read honor its own caller-requested deadline. Implemented, rebuilt, and re-verified live in a fresh container — it did NOT resolve the `GetLayout` disconnect (a different, now-understood root cause: the wire-marshalling gap above, not a timeout mismatch) and measurably increased overall CPU (the receive loop's existing "EOF and timeout both return `nil`, caller just retries" contract means a `recvmsg` returning immediately on a now-promptly-detected dead/EOF'd socket busy-spins the reader thread instead of being throttled by the stale, longer connect-time timeout). Reverted cleanly (confirmed via `git diff` against the intended fix) rather than ship an unproven, higher-risk change under time pressure — `git diff` after revert shows only the Hello/uniqueName/identity/GetGroupProperties changes, nothing else touched in `DBusConnection.swift`.

- 2026-09-07 [T-TRAY3] P0: fixed D76's flagged `GetLayout` connection-fatal disconnect. Root cause #1 (as diagnosed): `DBusValue.array([]).signatureCode` inferred "ay" for every empty array with no way to know the true element type; `GetLayout`'s root `properties: a{sv}`/leaf `children: av` are always empty. Fix: new `DBusValue.emptyArray(elementSignature:)` case + `.array(_:elementSignature:)` factory, applied at every reachable empty-array site (`DBusMenuLayoutBuilder`, `SingleInstanceDecision`, `ShowPickerOptions`). Root cause #2 (found while proving #1 with a real byte-level round trip, not just DBusValue-shape checks): `DBusByteWriter.write(.array(...))` built elements into an isolated sub-buffer and only aligned the splice point to the array element's OWN alignment — correct for STRUCT/DICT_ENTRY (max alignment 8) but wrong for VARIANT (declared 1, yet GetLayout's `av` wraps a STRUCT), silently desyncing every field after it. Fixed by writing array elements directly into the running buffer with a backpatched length (matching how STRUCT/DICT_ENTRY already worked), removing the buggy "isolated sub-buffer + splice" design entirely. Linux `swift build`+`swift test` 1106/1106 (1092+14 new), macOS 605/605/51 suites exact baseline untouched, `swift format lint --strict` clean. Real-bus proof: rebuilt `packaging/linux/vnc/Dockerfile` from this fix (`clipnest-vnc:trayfix`/`clipnest-desktop:trayfix`), real Ubuntu-Flashback `indicator-application-service`+`gnome-panel`: `dbus-send GetLayout` clean `METHOD_RETURN` x2 (was disconnecting every time), panel log free of `LIBDBUSMENU-GLIB-WARNING`, **tray icon rendered and screenshotted for the first time this project has ever seen it**, menu clicked open (3 correct labels), "Open Clipnest"/"Settings…" both drove the real running process (picker + Settings screenshotted), "Quit Clipnest" cleanly exited last. One harness note (not a Clipnest bug): the icon needed one `gnome-panel` restart after clipnest registered to actually appear — looks like the indicator-applet's own load-order/refresh timing in this container, flagged not investigated. Docs updated same-change: `debian/README.source` Known gap #4, `StatusNotifierTray.swift` doc comment, `DBusMenuLayoutBuilder.swift` doc comment. → board:test (dev ceiling, no separate tester this session), decision D77 in project-context.md.

- 2026-09-07 [T-WB1] P0 SIGSEGV investigated to a definitive, evidence-backed conclusion: NOT fixable from this task's assigned files. Reproduced via a new raw python-xlib client (kept as a scratch artifact, not swift-test-able) that answers TARGETS ICCCM-correctly but writes the reply property onto its own window instead of the requestor's — 8-9/10 crash rate. Root-caused via Ubuntu 22.04's actual `apt-get source gtk4` (4.6.9/4.6.2) + matching `-dbgsym` packages + a fully-symbolicated live core-dump backtrace: `strcmp`←`g_str_equal(type=NULL,"ATOM")`←`gdk_x11_clipboard_request_targets_got_stream`←...←`g_main_loop_run` — zero Clipnest frames, matches the white-box report exactly. Confirmed via source (`gdk/x11/gdkdisplay-x11.c`) that GTK's own CLIPBOARD+PRIMARY tracking is created UNCONDITIONALLY at `_gdk_x11_display_open` (the instant `gtk_init()` runs), with no supported opt-out (`gdk/gdkdebug.h`'s full flag table checked — `GDK_DEBUG_CLIPBOARD`/`_SELECTION` are logging-only). Confirmed via deepwiki against upstream GNOME/gtk that this exact line was fixed (`g_str_equal`→`g_strcmp0`) around GTK 4.10 (commit 0212291a) — past what Ubuntu 22.04's archives carry. So the real fix is a GTK4 dependency version bump (PPA/backport or build-from-source), a packaging/infra decision outside `Sources/ClipnestPlatformLinux/Clipboard/**`/`ClipnestGTKApplication.swift` — split out as new task T-WB1-GTKBUMP for architect/devops, escalated per the P0 routing rule (no relayed consent needed to say "I could not fix this in scope"). What WAS fixed, real and in-scope: `X11State.readProperty` had the exact same-class latent bug in Clipnest's OWN ICCCM read path (`actualType==None` indistinguishable from a real empty property) — silently defeating `gtkApplicationID(of:)`/`windowName(of:)` for any window lacking that property (`Optional("")` instead of `nil`, breaking `LinuxFrontmostAppReferenceProvider`'s fallback chain for most non-GTK4 apps); fixed with an explicit `actualType != Self.zero` guard. Also corrected `X11ClipboardConnection.swift`'s false "the one file that calls Xlib/XFixes" doc comment to document GTK's own independent tracking. Linux `swift build`+`swift test`: build clean, 1158 tests / 1157 passed, 1 pre-existing unrelated flake (ClipboardMonitorTests T-PERF3, cross-platform, host-load-sensitive — clean on isolated re-run). macOS `swift test` 605/605, 51 suites, exact baseline. `swift format lint --strict` clean on both files. Runtime-verified own container (`clipnest-x11crash`, port 6099, `packaging/linux/vnc/Dockerfile`-matching build): rebuilt release binary (`-c release -Xswiftc -static-stdlib`, matching `debian/rules`) STILL crashes 8/10 — reported honestly rather than claiming a fix that doesn't exist. Normal text/image/file capture + SQLite store integrity reconfirmed intact throughout. → board:blocked (T-WB1) + new board:todo (T-WB1-GTKBUMP, architect), decision D78 in project-context.md, escalated to user.
- 2026-09-07 [T-OCR9/T-OCR10] Shipped OCR on Linux end to end: fixed both pipeline bugs against pinned upstream references, vendored the real PP-OCRv5 weights, fixed packaging, and proved it live. **T-OCR9 (CTCDecoder drops spaces):** root cause was `CharacterDictionary`, not `CTCDecoder` — `dict.txt` (18383 lines) never included the trailing space class `rec.onnx` (18385 classes) was trained with under PaddleOCR's `use_space_char=True` (`ppocr/postprocess/rec_postprocess.py`: `BaseRecLabelDecode.__init__` appends `" "` to the dict AFTER its own chars, `CTCLabelDecode.add_special_char` then prepends `blank`; confirmed verbatim via deepwiki against PaddlePaddle/PaddleOCR). Fixed in `CharacterDictionary.load(path:)` via new `appendingSpaceCharacter(to:)` — `CTCDecoder` itself needed zero changes, its bounds check was already correct for whatever dictionary it's given. **T-OCR10 (BoxOrdering under-sorts):** `sortReadingOrder` did one forward adjacent-pair bubble pass; PaddleOCR's real `sorted_boxes` (`tools/infer/predict_system.py`, confirmed verbatim via deepwiki) is a BACKWARD insertion-sort pass with an early break, keyed on `topLeft` directly (not `min(topLeft,topRight)`/`min(topLeft,bottomLeft)` the old code used). Reproduced exactly, including a docstring copy of the literal Python. Vendored the real, checksum-verified PP-OCRv5 weights (RapidOCR ModelScope conversion, tag v3.9.2 — all 4 SHA-256s matched the POC-pinned values exactly) into `packaging/linux/vendor/ppocr-models/`, wrote `SOURCE.md`+`fetch.sh` mirroring the onnxruntime vendor dir's pattern, added a real `Files: packaging/linux/vendor/ppocr-models/*` Apache-2.0 stanza to `debian/copyright` (verified via GitHub's license API against both PaddlePaddle/PaddleOCR and RapidAI/RapidOCR; initially embedded the full license text, `lintian` flagged `copyright-file-contains-full-apache-2-license`/`copyright-not-using-common-license-for-apache2` — fixed by referencing Debian's common-licenses copy instead, re-verified `lintian ../*.deb` now 0 errors, only 2 pre-existing unrelated onnxruntime-SONAME warnings outside this task's scope). `packaging/linux/vnc/Dockerfile`: removed the zero-byte model stand-in block (real files now flow through `COPY . /src`) and broadened the runtime stage's install glob (`clipnest_*.deb` -> `clipnest*.deb`) so the VNC test image actually installs clipnest-ocr/clipnest-ocr-data, not just clipnest. Settings honesty fix: added `OnnxTextRecognizer.isAvailable` (dlopen + model-locate check), threaded a new `isTextRecognitionAvailable: Bool` through `SettingsWindow`/`LinuxAppEnvironment` (both files concurrently being edited by T-OPT3 for an unrelated Permissions tab — added my parameter as a minimal, isolated insertion after the existing `ocrBackfillViewModel` param, confirmed a clean merge via a real build), `SettingsWindow+History.swift` now hides the "Recognize text in copied images" toggle + Fast/Accurate picker and shows a plain "Install clipnest-ocr..." label when OCR truly isn't installed, reusing the window's own established "statement instead of a no-op control" idiom. Fixed stale hard-link/compile-flag doc language in `debian/README.source` (Known gap #3 marked RESOLVED) and `debian/rules`' header comment, per the DONE gate's docs-in-sync requirement. Tests: extended `OCRCharacterDictionaryTests.swift` (space-append unit tests + a CTCDecoder integration test proving "brown fox jumps" no longer collapses), new `Tests/ClipnestPlatformLinuxTests/OCRBoxOrderingTests.swift` (synthetic geometry proving the OLD single-bubble-pass algorithm mis-orders a box needing a 2-3 position leftward correction, matching the task's own measured "quick brown fox" -> "quick, fox, brown" symptom, hand-traced both algorithms before writing the fixtures). New dedicated target `Tests/ClipnestLinuxOCRTests/` (added to `Package.swift`, deliberate split from `ClipnestPlatformLinuxTests` since it shells out to `convert`) with `OCRRealImageFixtureTests.swift`: real ImageMagick-rendered PNGs through the REAL vendored ONNX Runtime + PP-OCRv5, `multiWordTextKeepsSpacesEndToEnd` ("brown fox jumps" -> recognized exactly "brown fox jumps") and `multiLineTextRecognizesInReadingOrder` ("alpha\nomega" -> recognized exactly "alpha\nomega"); both conditional/graceful-skip outside a fully-provisioned container, matching `OCREndToEndRealModelTests`'s established shape. Full verification in own Docker container/image (`clipnest-ocr-dev` dev loop, `clipnest-vnc:ocr9` packaging image, `clipnest-ocr-verify` container, port 6098 — all my own, never touched another agent's): Linux `swift test` **1158/1158, 0 failures** (includes my new tests + `OCREndToEndRealModelTests`'s pre-existing real-model test, now actually exercised since real models are vendored); macOS `swift test` **605 tests in 51 suites, exact baseline, unaffected**; `swift format lint --strict` clean on every touched file (2 line-length nits auto-fixed). `dpkg-buildpackage -us -uc -b -d` produces all 3 real debs — `clipnest-ocr-data_0.9.1_all.deb` is **19,571,854 bytes** (was empty/placeholder before this task), verified via `dpkg-deb -c` to contain all 4 real model files at the correct sizes. Installed all 3 debs in a real running container, enabled OCR via `~/.config/clipnest/settings.json` (default is off), copied 6 real ImageMagick-rendered images to the X11 clipboard one at a time, and read `ocr_text` back from `/root/.local/share/Clipnest/ClipItems.sqlite3` via `python3 -c "import sqlite3..."` (no `sqlite3` CLI in the minimal runtime image) — every one matched exactly: "Clipnest OCR works", "Second latency test 42", "Third latency check 99", "Latency trial 1/2/3 sample", and "top row\nbottom row" (multi-line, correct order). Measured latency (same-clock instrumentation, clipboard-write to `ocr_text`-populated): 5 single-line trials 0.42-0.85s, one two-line capture 1.51s — real numbers on this hardware (Docker Desktop's aarch64 Linux VM on Apple Silicon, disclosed as such, not claimed as representative of bare-metal x86 Ubuntu). One real, unrelated build-infra failure hit along the way and fixed: a corrupted llbuild SQLite build database (`Assertion 'result == SQLITE_OK' failed`) from my own earlier interrupted background builds — cleared `.build/aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu/release` (gitignored cache dir) and rebuilt clean. `git add` done for every owned file including the 3 `.onnx` binaries and `dict.txt` — no commit. Decision D80 in project-context.md. Dev ceiling `status:test` — board updated directly (this session's own pen-holder, no spawner) → reviewer then tester next; mandatory security pass still owed given this touches Settings/user-input-adjacent capture (flagging explicitly, not skipping silently).
- 2026-09-07 [T-RTF1] Closed the Linux rich-text fidelity gap vs. macOS, evidence-first. Ground truth via a real, self-provisioned container (`clipnest-rtf`, VNC port 6120, Ubuntu 22.04 + LibreOffice Writer 7.3.7 + gedit + epiphany-browser): `xclip -t TARGETS -o` on a bold/plain copy from LibreOffice Writer shows it offers BOTH `text/html` AND `text/rtf` (no `image/png` thumbnail target, ruling out my initial cross-category-priority hypothesis). Static analysis of `MimeRepresentationSelector.winningMimeType`/`LinuxPasteboard.data(forType: .rtf)` confirmed the actual bug: capture picked only the single highest-priority rich-text MIME type (`text/html` wins `LinuxClipboardConstants.richTextMimePriority`) and silently discarded whichever other representation(s) the source also offered; `GTKClipboardWriting.writeRichText` then only ever republished that one relabeled blob as `text/html` on paste — never advertising a second format for a receiving app to choose. Fix stays entirely inside owned files, zero `ClipnestCore`/Store changes (macOS frozen, verified): new `Sources/ClipnestPlatformLinux/Clipboard/LinuxRichTextBundle.swift` — a pure, self-describing wire format (magic `CNR1` + count + [mimeType,data] entries) that packs every rich-text representation the owner offers into the ONE opaque blob slot `ClipnestCore` already has room for (`Classification.rawData`/`ClipItem.blobPath` never changed shape). `LinuxPasteboard.data(forType: .rtf)` now calls new `MimeRepresentationSelector.allAvailableMimeTypes(for:in:)` (returns every present candidate, not just the winner) and bundles all of them — also more resilient than before (a failed payload fetch for one candidate no longer aborts the whole capture). `GTKClipboardWriting.writeRichText` decodes the bundle via new pure `representationsToPublish(for:)` and republishes every representation under its own real MIME type in one GDK union provider; falls back to treating undecodable bytes as flat `text/html` so every pre-fix history item still pastes exactly as before (no migration). Updated the one stale doc comment this fixes (`GTKClipboardWriting.swift`'s "known limitation... out of scope" paragraph, DONE-gate item 5). 21 new unit tests (`LinuxRichTextBundleTests` round-trip/malformed-input/binary-payload coverage, `MimeRepresentationSelectorTests`/`LinuxPasteboardTests` multi-representation + partial-conversion-failure coverage, `GTKClipboardWritingTests` pure decode/fallback coverage) + 1 existing test's assertion updated for the new bundle contract (`LinuxClipboardSelectionReplacerTests`, snapshot/restore now decodes-and-compares instead of pinning raw bytes). Verified in total isolation from 4 other agents' concurrent uncommitted WIP on this shared tree: `git worktree add --detach HEAD` + only my 9 files overlaid, `swift test` → **1179/1179** (1158 baseline + 21 new), zero failures — including the pre-existing host-load-sensitive `ClipboardMonitorTests` T-PERF3 flake (D55), which failed once under full-suite contention on this heavily-loaded shared Docker host and then passed cleanly on 2 isolated re-runs, confirming it's unrelated to this change. macOS `swift test` exact **605/51** and `xcodebuild test` exact **48/9**, both re-run this session, byte-for-byte unchanged (zero macOS-relevant files touched). `swift format lint --recursive --strict` clean on all 9 touched files. Real end-to-end proof (own container, own binary, not the .deb): LibreOffice Writer → Clipnest → new LibreOffice Writer document round-trips bold formatting correctly (screenshotted); `xclip -t TARGETS -o` on Clipnest's own pasted-back clipboard now lists BOTH `text/html` and `text/rtf`, each byte-identical to LibreOffice's original payloads (the literal "paste TARGETS proof" this task asked for); gedit (plain-text-only) receives the correct stripped plain text with zero leaked markup (screenshotted) — proves the single/no-rich-representation fallback path is unaffected. Browser (epiphany) round-trip was attempted but not completed live: WebKitGTK needed `WEBKIT_DISABLE_SANDBOX_THIS_IS_DANGEROUS=1` to launch at all under this container's `bwrap` restrictions, and the shared host's severe concurrent-agent CPU/IO contention wedged this container's X11/Xvfb session (repeated `wmctrl`/`xdotool` timeouts, `Xvfb` itself ending up defunct) partway through — reported honestly rather than faked. Not a correctness gap: a browser's html-only, no-RTF copy is the exact single-representation-bundle path already covered by `richTextOnlySourceCapturedWithRealMimeType`/`fallsBackToFlatHTMLForLegacyBlob`-style tests, and the identical `text/html` bytes already round-tripped correctly via xclip and via the strictly-harder LibreOffice multi-representation case above. Honest remaining gap, disclosed not overclaimed: `text/richtext` (RFC 1896, seen in LibreOffice's TARGETS list) and any OLE/embedded-object formats a source might also offer are NOT captured — deliberately out of scope (obsolete/rare formats with no real modern consumer; chasing them would be scope creep past "close the html vs. rtf gap" this task named). `NullRichTextFlattener` (preview-text flattening) is unchanged/still a no-op on Linux — untouched, per this task's directive not to touch `ClipnestCore`, and unaffected in practice since `fallbackPlainText` already covers every real source tested. Container `clipnest-rtf` (VNC port 6120) left running for follow-up inspection. `git add` done for all 9 files, no commit. Dev ceiling `status:test` per integrity rule 2 — no separate reviewer/tester spawned this session (same disclosed precedent as T-OPT3/T-TRAY3/T-OCR9) → handing to reviewer then tester next.
- 2026-09-07 [T-BB4/T-BB5/T-BB7] Routed black-box follow-up, three items. **Preview panel (new T-BB7):** never runtime-verified end to end before — verified in own container (`clipnest-previewfix-dev`, plain Xvfb via `docker exec`/`import`, own `swift build --product clipnest` binary, no VNC needed) against real captured text/image/file items, screenshotted per kind and cross-checked against macOS's `ItemPreview.swift`. Found and fixed 3 content gaps (`.image` showed a stray `previewText` caption macOS never shows; `.file` was missing size+path — size read live via `FileManager`, matching `PasteboardReader.readFile`'s own doc comment that `ClipItem.byteSize` is always 0 for `.file` by design; the T-OCR2 "Recognized Text" section had no Linux widget at all) plus a real GTK4 rendering bug (a correctly-decoded 240×160 pixbuf — confirmed via temporary, since-reverted `gdk_pixbuf_get_width/height` diagnostics — rendered as a ~16×9px speck; fixed with `gtk_image_set_pixel_size(previewImage, max(w,h))`, empirically necessary on this GTK 4.6.9 build despite that API's own docs describing it as icon-name-only). New pure `Support/ItemPreviewContent.swift` (mirrors `ClipItemRowContent`'s split) + `PickerWindow.swift`/`+Layout.swift`/`+Preview.swift` updated; one new `GTKShims.swift` shim (`gtk_separator_new`, same file's existing convention). 9 new tests. Linux `swift test` 1195/1196 (1 failure is `LinuxClipboardSelectionReplacerTests`, outside this task's file scope — another agent's concurrent work, confirmed unrelated by file-scope). `swift format lint --strict` clean on every touched/new file except `GTKShims.swift`'s pre-existing whole-file `AlwaysUseLowerCamelCase` condition (its own `swift-format-ignore-file` pragma doesn't suppress `lint --strict` on this toolchain; not caused by my one added line, which matches the file's ~70 pre-existing identically-shaped lines). **T-BB4 (retention default):** verdict — code is correct (1000-item default), docs were stale. `SettingsStore` is shared macOS+Linux; D50 (2026-09-04, predates this task) already treated "the default 1000-item retention cap" as established fact, proving this was never a Linux-only accident. Verified live on a genuinely fresh Linux profile (`~/.config/clipnest/` confirmed absent, Settings opened via `gdbus call ... app.clipnest.Control.OpenSettings`, screenshotted): "Limit by item count: 1000" pre-selected. Fixed `docs/usage.md`'s retention section to state the real default using macOS's actual picker-label wording; no code change. **T-BB5 (paint delay):** diagnosed with reproduced, timestamped screenshot evidence (own container); nothing changed in `PickerWindow+Reconcile.swift`/`PickerWindowRefreshCoalescer.swift` because neither is the cause. Two distinct root causes: (1) the Clear-All dialog gap is a pure GTK/Xvfb first-paint/compositor artifact — `showConfirmationDialog` (not owned) builds all content synchronously before `gtk_window_present()`; reproduced via timestamped sequence, WM titlebar instant, client content blank through t+450ms, rendered by t+744ms, no code-level gap possible. (2) tab-switch/search-clear lag is real async-fetch latency in `PickerViewModel`/`PagedQuery` (`ClipnestViewModels`) gated on `GTKMainActorBridge`'s 8ms `DispatchQueue.main`-emulation pump (`ClipnestLinuxAppKit`, neither owned) — traced precisely (`activeTab`'s didSet fires the tab-button update instantly via this task's own coalescer, correctly; the ROW data lands 100-400ms later via a `Task` that must round-trip that pump) and reproduced (pin + tab-switch showed stale rows through ~t+300ms). Both root-cause files are outside this task's scope and are a recently-hardened subsystem (fixed "the single most dangerous item in the Linux port," per its own doc comments) — flagged as an architect/PM follow-up rather than patched. Full mechanism, all timestamps, and the exact evidence for both diagnoses: D84/D85/D86 in project-context.md. **Disclosed incident:** a `pkill` aimed at my own now-unneeded VNC image build (`docker build -f packaging/linux/vnc/Dockerfile ...`) also matched and canceled a different concurrent agent's `docker build -t clipnest-build:kbparity` — that agent's T-KBPARITY2 board entry already shows full evidence/`status:test` recorded from before this incident, so no unrecorded work appears lost, but flagging for that agent/PM. Container `clipnest-previewfix-dev` left running. `git add` done on all touched/new files (2 source dirs + `GTKShims.swift` + `docs/usage.md` + 1 new test file), no commit. Board updated directly (this session's own pen-holder for this routed request). Dev ceiling `status:test` on all three (T-BB4/T-BB5/T-BB7) — no separate reviewer/tester spawned this pass → reviewer then tester next.
- 2026-09-07 [T-KBPARITY2] Closed Linux's 4-shortcut keyboard-parity gap (save-as-snippet/new-snippet/edit-snippet/open-settings all mouse-only before this) and added the regression coverage an independent reviewer correctly flagged as missing from the earlier P0 Delete fix (T-BB1/commit 8642aee had zero automated coverage). Bindings, `KeyEventMapping.action(keyval:state:)`: `Ctrl+S`→`.saveAsSnippet`, `Ctrl+N`→`.newSnippet`, `Ctrl+Shift+E`→`.replaceSnippet` (needs both `controlMask` and `shiftMask` — verified at runtime, not assumed, since the task flagged this chord specifically), `Ctrl+,`→`.openSettings`; all written as plain `case`+ternary (never `case a,b where cond:`) per this file's own documented hazard. `PickerWindow+Keyboard.swift` dispatch mirrors macOS's exact gating from `PickerView.handle(_:)`: `.saveAsSnippet` ungated (`saveHighlightedAsSnippet()` self-guards), `.newSnippet` gated `activeTab == .snippets` at the dispatch site (`presentCreateSnippetForm()` has no internal guard), `.openSettings` calls `viewModel.openSettingsFromPicker()` unconditionally. `.replaceSnippet` has no macOS *key* analogue at all (macOS's ⌥⌘E is `SnippetExpander`'s unrelated global OS-text-selection-replace hotkey) — it maps to the same `presentEditSnippetForm(_:)` a Snippets-row's mouse "Edit" button already calls, resolving the highlighted snippet via the already-public `selectedSnippetID`/`snippetRows` (mirrors `PickerView.swift`'s own `previewTargetID` lookup pattern) rather than adding a new `PickerViewModel` method or touching that file at all. `viewModel.openSettings` was never wired on Linux (only macOS's `PickerView.onAppear` set it) — added the one closure in `LinuxAppEnvironment.init` to the same `openSettings()` the tray/D-Bus entry already calls; without it `Ctrl+,` would have dismissed the picker and done nothing. `ShortcutHints.swift`'s Linux `platformDefault`: `save`/`newSnippet`/`replaceSnippet`/`settings` flipped from `nil` to `"Ctrl+S save"`/`"Ctrl+N new"`/`"Ctrl+Shift+E replace"`/`"Ctrl+, settings"` — macOS branch untouched, confirmed byte-identical (`swift test --filter ShortcutHints` on macOS host: same 20/2 pass as before edit). `LinuxShortcutDescriptions.swift` updated to match exactly (previously-reported T-BB6 class of defect: two in-app shortcut lists disagreeing). Reviewer-requested regression coverage: extracted `PickerWindow+Keyboard.swift`'s private `isTypingInSearchField` (untestable — needs a live GTK widget tree) into `static func shouldPropagateToSearchEntry(action:focusIsInSearchEntry:searchText:)`, pure/3-plain-values-in-1-Bool-out, called from `handleKeyPressed` with the two live GTK reads it still needs; behavior unchanged (verified: same 2-guard structure, same P0 fix still lands). New `GTKPickerWindowKeyboardTests.swift` pins the full matrix the reviewer named: delete+focus+non-empty→propagate, delete+focus+empty→act (the exact "focus-only regresses bare Delete" case a first attempt at the P0 fix got wrong), delete+no-focus→act regardless of text, and every non-delete action (including all 4 new ones) never propagates in any focus/text combination. New `GTKPickerKeyActionTests.swift` pins `isTextEditingKeyWhenTypingInSearch`: true only for `.delete`, false for every other case including the 4 additions. - 2026-09-07 [T-KBPARITY3] Closed the last architectural gap between Linux's and macOS's picker key handling: macOS's `PickerView.swift` attaches `.onKeyPress` to the OUTER container so it only sees keys the focused `TextField` did NOT consume (bubble semantics); GTK's `GtkEventControllerKey` on `PickerWindow` runs at `GTK_PHASE_CAPTURE` (the opposite order, required so the picker's own chords work regardless of focus). T-KBPARITY2's fix approximated the difference for `.delete` with `searchText.isEmpty` — right for an empty box, wrong for "caret at the end of non-empty text" (macOS bubbles a no-op forward-delete to the picker; the old Linux check always forwarded to the entry whenever the box had text, so GTK rang its error bell and swallowed it).
  **Investigated the "natural" fix first — a second `GTK_PHASE_BUBBLE` controller on the window — and found it genuinely can't work, verified not assumed**: built a throwaway GTK4 C probe (`GtkSearchEntry`, one `GTK_PHASE_CAPTURE` + one `GTK_PHASE_BUBBLE` `GtkEventControllerKey` on the window, real Ubuntu 22.04 `libgtk-4-dev` 4.6.9 under Xvfb, driven by `xdotool`) and confirmed via `gtk_editable_get_selection_bounds`/`get_position` state logging: `GtkText`'s Delete key binding reports itself HANDLED (stopping propagation) in every state tested — mid-text (after a real delete), caret at the end of non-empty text (a no-op, `gtk_widget_error_bell` rings but the event still doesn't propagate), and a completely empty entry (also swallowed). A literal bubble controller therefore NEVER sees Delete while the entry has focus, in ANY state — it would regress the already-shipped "empty search + Delete acts on the picker" behavior (T-BB1), not fix the end-of-text case. Also confirmed the reverse for the other direction: the same probe showed a `GTK_PHASE_BUBBLE` controller DOES still see `Ctrl+S`/`Ctrl+N`/`Ctrl+Shift+E`/`Ctrl+,` while the entry has focus — a plain `GtkSearchEntry` never consumes any of the 4 keyboard-parity chords (the task's specific worry about `Ctrl+Shift+E`/IME interception didn't materialize in this environment) — so leaving those 6-plus non-delete actions in `GTK_PHASE_CAPTURE` (unchanged) stays correct and doesn't need touching.
  **Closest safe equivalent, implemented**: kept the single `GTK_PHASE_CAPTURE` controller/architecture (unchanged), but replaced the pure decision's third parameter — `PickerWindow.shouldPropagateToSearchEntry(action:focusIsInSearchEntry:searchText:)` → `...searchEntryEditWouldHaveEffect:)` (`Sources/ClipnestGTK/Window/PickerWindow+Keyboard.swift`) — with the exact predicate `GtkText`'s own `gtk_text_delete_from_cursor` uses internally: an active selection, or a character sitting after the caret. New private `searchEntryDeleteWouldHaveEffect(_:)` reads the two live GTK values (`gtk_editable_get_selection_bounds`, `gtk_editable_get_position` vs. `g_utf8_strlen` of the entry's text — NOT `String.count`, which counts grapheme clusters and can disagree with GTK's per-codepoint count for combining sequences/emoji) at the one call site that already reads live GTK state (`gtkFocusIsWithin`). The pure function itself is unchanged in shape (3 plain values in, 1 `Bool` out, no GTK dependency) so it stays exhaustively unit-testable; only its third input's MEANING got more precise. `PickerKeyAction.isTextEditingKeyWhenTypingInSearch`'s doc comment updated to point at the finer-grained decision, no behavior change there (still only `.delete`).
  Tests: rewrote `GTKPickerWindowKeyboardTests.swift`'s full matrix for the new parameter — mid-text/selection→propagate, caret-at-end-of-non-empty-text→act (the exact new case, previously untested and previously wrong), empty-text→act (regression guard, unchanged outcome), no-focus→act regardless, every non-delete action→never propagates (including the 4 keyboard-parity additions). `searchEntryDeleteWouldHaveEffect(_:)` itself is intentionally NOT unit-tested (needs a live GTK widget tree/display, same reasoning `gtkFocusIsWithin` isn't) — covered by the runtime VNC proof below instead.
  Docs (DONE gate item 5): found `docs/API-ClipnestGTK.md`'s keyboard table was ALREADY stale before this task even started — it referenced the long-since-renamed `isTypingInSearchField` and never listed the 4 T-KBPARITY2 chords at all (a T-KBPARITY2 doc gap, not mine, but in the exact area I'm touching so fixed now) — added the 4 missing rows, rewrote the Delete row + a new scenario table matching this log's evidence exactly, and documented the `GTK_PHASE_BUBBLE` investigation's negative result so it isn't silently re-attempted later. `LinuxShortcutDescriptions.swift`'s user-facing Delete description ("...when the search box is empty...") was also now inaccurate (T-KBPARITY2 already left it slightly imprecise) — reworded to "unless it would edit the search box instead" (condition-agnostic, won't go stale at the next refinement); updated `GTKLinuxShortcutDescriptionsTests.swift`'s matching assertion (both green).
  **Scenario-by-scenario table (macOS column is INFERRED from `PickerView.swift`'s own doc comments/tests, not observed — cannot drive the Mac app interactively; Linux column is OBSERVED, both via unit tests and the runtime proof below):**
  | Scenario | macOS (inferred) | Linux (observed) |
  |---|---|---|
  | Caret mid-text, Delete | Field forward-deletes a char; picker never sees it | Same — verified live: "abc"+caret@1+Delete -> box shows "ab", DB unaffected |
  | Caret at END of non-empty text, Delete | Not consumed; bubbles; picker deletes highlighted item | Same — verified live: box "kbparity3-clip-1" (caret@end) + Delete -> box UNCHANGED, DB 4->3, exactly that row gone |
  | Empty search, Delete | Same as above (empty = "caret at the end") degenerate case | Same — verified live: empty box + Delete -> DB 3->2, no regression from T-BB1 |
  | Active selection in search box, Delete | Field deletes the selection; picker never sees it | Same — verified live: Ctrl+A then Delete -> box emptied, DB unaffected |
  | List/row focused (not search entry), Delete | N/A on macOS today (search field always focused, per `PickerView.swift`'s own doc comment) | Picker always deletes the highlighted item — verified live (clicked a row's Pin button, pressed Delete, the HIGHLIGHTED row was deleted, not the one clicked) + exhaustive unit coverage for `focusIsInSearchEntry: false` |
  | Ctrl+S/N/Shift+E/, while typing | Cmd-chords always act (TextField never binds them) | Same — probe confirms GtkText never consumes any of the 4; live Ctrl+P-while-typing re-confirmed a chord isn't swallowed and the box text is untouched |
  | Arrows/Escape/Return while typing | Unaffected by this task (macOS's field doesn't bind Up/Down/Esc; Return has its own `.onSubmit` belt-and-suspenders) | Unaffected — `dispatch(_:)` and every non-delete `KeyEventMapping` case are byte-for-byte untouched by this diff (verified via `git diff`) |
  **What GTK genuinely cannot match**: real GTK4 event bubbling for Delete specifically (see investigation above) — the fix reproduces the same OBSERVABLE outcome by computing the equivalent predicate at capture time instead, not by using GTK's own propagation mechanism, which cannot do it.
  Verification: macOS `swift test` exact **605 tests / 51 suites** (unaffected — every touched file is Linux-only). Linux, own isolated dev container (`clipnest-kbparity3-dev`, `swift:6.0-jammy`+`libgtk-4-dev`, tar-copied tree snapshot): full-tree `swift build` + `swift test --parallel` -> **1233/1233 passing** (grown past the 1201 baseline from concurrent parallel-agent work on this shared tree — confirmed by `git status`/`git diff --stat` that 3 other files with in-flight WIP, `SettingsWindow+General.swift`/`SettingsWindow.swift`/`LinuxAppEnvironment.swift` — another agent's Linux-updater task, NOT touched by me, explicitly out of my file scope — needed 3 small CONTAINER-ONLY workarounds, never applied to the real tree, to get a clean build snapshot for verifying the REST of the tree; confirmed zero `CONTAINER-ONLY` strings leaked into the host files via grep before `git add`); targeted `--filter GTKPickerWindowKeyboardTests`/`GTKPickerKeyActionTests`/`GTKLinuxShortcutDescriptionsTests` all independently green (9+2+4). `swift format lint --recursive --strict Sources Tests` clean on all 5 touched files (grepped full-tree output for each filename — zero hits). Runtime-proven on a real installed `.deb` from the official `packaging/linux/vnc/Dockerfile` (own image `clipnest-vnc:kbparity3`, own container `clipnest-kbparity3`, port 6155, left running) — every row in the scenario table above marked "verified live" was screenshotted and cross-checked against `sqlite3 /root/.local/share/Clipnest/ClipItems.sqlite3`'s live row count/content, not just observed visually.
  Files (all within this task's owned scope): `Sources/ClipnestGTK/Window/PickerWindow+Keyboard.swift`, `Sources/ClipnestGTK/Support/PickerKeyAction.swift`, `Tests/ClipnestPlatformLinuxTests/GTKPickerWindowKeyboardTests.swift`; adjacent doc-accuracy-only touches (DONE gate item 5, not functional): `Sources/ClipnestGTK/Support/LinuxShortcutDescriptions.swift`, `Tests/ClipnestPlatformLinuxTests/GTKLinuxShortcutDescriptionsTests.swift`, `docs/API-ClipnestGTK.md`. Did not touch `ClipnestApp/**`, `Sources/ClipnestPlatformLinux/**`, `Sources/ClipnestLinuxAppKit/App/**`, `SettingsWindow*`, or `PickerWindow.dismiss()`/`isDismissing`, per assignment. `git add` done on all 6 files, no commit. No separate reviewer/tester spawned this session (same disclosed precedent as T-KBPARITY1/2, T-OPT1/3) — dev ceiling `status:test` per integrity rule 2, reporting up for board write since no spawner context exists this session.
- 2026-09-07 [T-LXUPD] Gave Linux the same self-update capability macOS has, per the port plan's Phase 4 spec ("`UpdateChecker` ports unchanged; `AppUpdater` branches on provenance: PPA → show the apt command; raw `.deb` → checksum-verified download + `pkexec apt-get install`"). Read macOS's real `AppUpdater.swift` first (it shells a curl|bash script to Terminal via `NSWorkspace`, not an in-app downloader — mirrored its doc-comment style, its "silent fallback on failure, never auto-install" contract, and its "pure logic unit-tested, real Process/network calls integration-verified separately" split, not its literal Terminal-script mechanism, which has no Linux analogue). New `Sources/ClipnestLinuxAppKit/App/LinuxAppUpdater.swift`: `detectProvenance()` runs real `apt-cache policy <package>` and parses whether the INSTALLED (`***`) version's origin block contains anything besides the literal `/var/lib/dpkg/status` — verified for real in a throwaway `ubuntu:22.04` container in BOTH states (`dpkg -i` with no repo configured → `*** 0.9.1 100` / `100 /var/lib/dpkg/status` only; adding a local `file:` apt source serving the same package → a second, higher-priority origin line appears ahead of it) before writing the parser, not after. Found and fixed a real bug in my own first draft while writing the "uninstalled candidate version at a different indentation" regression test: comparing origin-line indentation against the INSTALLED line's own indent (1 space) misreads a following candidate-version line (5 spaces — more than 1, but a different kind of line, not an origin) as a fabricated non-dpkg-status "origin," which would have wrongly reported `.packageManaged` for the case "raw .deb install installed, but the PPA also carries a different/older version." Fixed by anchoring on the exact indentation of the FIRST line after the installed one (verified 8 spaces in both real captures) and only consuming further lines at that identical indentation. `performUpdate(installedVersion:onStep:)`: real `curl` (same `/usr/bin/curl`+`Process`+`--max-time`/`--connect-timeout` pattern `UpdateChecker.fetchLatestReleaseJSON()` already established — a permitted extension of the app's one deliberate network exception, never `URLSession`) fetches the release JSON, selects the `.deb` asset matching this machine's real `dpkg --print-architecture`/`/etc/os-release` `VERSION_CODENAME` (excludes `clipnest-ocr`/`clipnest-ocr-data` siblings by construction — `hasPrefix("clipnest_")` never matches `"clipnest-ocr_..."`), fetches its published `.sha256`, downloads the `.deb`, hashes it via the EXISTING canonical `BlobStore.contentHash(of:)` (`ClipnestCore` — reused, not reimplemented), and refuses to proceed (`.noChecksumPublished`/`.checksumMismatch`) on anything but an exact match — checksum verification runs unconditionally, before `pkexec` is ever spawned. Only then: `pkexec apt-get install -y <path>` (bare `apt-get`, not an absolute path — pkexec resolves non-absolute commands against its own secure PATH by design, confirmed via real-world `pkexec` usage conventions; `-y` only silences apt's own redundant re-confirmation prompt, not a second consent gate — the two real gates are the Settings "Install Update…" confirmation dialog and polkit's own auth dialog inside this exact invocation). Exit-code handling is based on `pkexec(1)`'s OWN documented contract (freedesktop.org/Ubuntu manpages: exit 126 = "authorization not obtained/dismissed"), verified for real (not assumed) against a real `policykit-1` in a container: `pkexec /bin/true` as a non-root user with no authority connection → 127; with a real `dbus-daemon`+real `/usr/libexec/polkitd` running but no auth agent/controlling tty → STILL 127 ("Error creating textual authentication agent... No such device or address"), never 126 in this headless shape — so only 126 maps to `.installationCancelled`; everything else (including the empirically-observed 127) surfaces as `.installationFailed(<real captured stderr>)`, never swallowed — deliberately did NOT copy `GrantInputHelperClient`'s doc-comment claim of "2 the user dismissed" (unverified for bare `pkexec`, likely describes that helper script's own exit convention, not `pkexec`'s).
  UI: `SettingsWindow+General.swift` (below "Automatically check for updates") gets an installed-version line, a "Check for Updates Now" button (calls the SAME `updateChecker.checkNow()` the 24h background timer uses — no second implementation), and, only once `updateChecker.isUpdateAvailable` is true, a provenance-branched block: `.packageManaged(origin:)` shows the real origin + a selectable (`gtk_label_set_selectable`, not a `GtkEntry` — avoids inviting edits to a command that must run verbatim) copy-pasteable `sudo apt update && sudo apt install --only-upgrade clipnest`, no install button; `.standaloneDebInstall` shows "Install Update…" gated behind `showConfirmationDialog(...)` (same mandatory-confirmation shape as History's "Clear All History…") before `performLinuxAppUpdate` ever runs; `.undetermined(reason:)` shows the real reason + the public releases URL, fail-safe not fail-open. `LinuxUpdateProvenance`/`LinuxUpdateStep`/`LinuxUpdateOutcome`/`LinuxAppUpdaterError`/`UpdateSettingsPresentation` live in `SettingsWindow+General.swift` (`ClipnestGTK`), not `LinuxAppUpdater.swift` (`ClipnestLinuxAppKit`) — same module-layering reason `UInputPermissionStatus`/`UInputGrantOutcome` live in `SettingsWindow+Permissions.swift` (`ClipnestGTK` sits below `ClipnestLinuxAppKit`, cannot import it); `LinuxAppUpdater` imports `ClipnestGTK` purely to construct/return them, mirroring `UInputPermissionChecker`/`GrantInputHelperClient`'s established precedent exactly. `SettingsWindow.init` gained 4 REQUIRED, non-defaulted parameters (`installedVersionText`, `detectUpdateProvenance`, `performLinuxAppUpdate`, `aptUpgradeCommand`) — no default value on either closure, per coding-standards.md's silent-seam-default non-negotiable (the `presentSnippetEditor`/`openSettings` precedent this session already hit twice). Also closed a THIRD instance of that exact trap found while wiring this: `PickerViewModel.appVersion`/`requestAppUpdate` (shared, `ClipnestViewModels`) were declared with no-op defaults and never set anywhere on Linux — wired in `LinuxAppEnvironment.init` (`appVersion = installedVersion`; `requestAppUpdate` opens Settings' General tab, since no picker-footer UI exists yet to drive a richer flow — `PickerWindow*.swift` is explicitly another agent's owned files this session). `swift-format`'s import-visibility rule surfaced one real gap while wiring the copyable apt-command label: `gtk_label_set_selectable` needed `import CGtk4` added to `SettingsWindow+General.swift` (previously reached GTK only through `SettingsWindow+Controls.swift`'s shared module-internal shims, which need no per-file import).
  Verification (own container `clipnest-updatefeat-dev`, bind-mounted `swift:6.0-jammy`, own build-cache volume — never touched another agent's running container): Linux `swift build`/`swift test` on the real, unmodified working tree → **1246/1246 passing** (baseline 1201 + this task's 42 new tests: 29 in `AppLinuxAppUpdaterTests.swift` covering `AptPolicyParsing` against the two real captured transcripts above plus a multi-version-block regression for the indentation bug, `ReleaseAssetParsing`/`ChecksumFileParsing`/`OSReleaseParsing` pure logic, and 2 real-process tests honestly gated on this container actually having `apt-cache`/`dpkg`; 13 in `GTKUpdateSettingsPresentationTests.swift` for `UpdateSettingsPresentation`'s copy/gating). `swift format lint --recursive --strict` clean on all 6 touched/new files. macOS `swift test`: exact **605 tests / 51 suites**, re-confirmed unaffected (Linux-only files). Both provenance branches proven LIVE, not just unit-tested: real `clipnest` GTK binary (`swift build` debug, this exact repo state) run under Xvfb+a session `dbus-daemon`+openbox in the same container, `gdbus call ... app.clipnest.Control.OpenSettings` to open the real Settings window, `import -window root` screenshots. Forced `isUpdateAvailable=true` via a REAL `updateChecker.checkNow()` against the REAL public GitHub API (confirmed reachable + a real `v0.9.1` release exists) by temporarily setting `installedVersion` to `"0.0.1"` in a CONTAINER-LOCAL tar-copy of the repo (`/root/verify`, never the bind-mounted host tree — confirmed after the fact via `grep -rn "TEMP-VERIFY\|0\.0\.1" <the 4 real files>` on the host, zero matches) — this only patches the demonstration's forced-outdated-version input, not the provenance/checksum logic itself, which ran unmodified either way. Screenshot 1 (`clipnest` registered via a real `dpkg -i` of a throwaway control-only package, no apt source configured): "Update to v0.9.1 available — Installed from a downloaded .deb, not a repository — Clipnest can install this update itself." + a live "Install Update…" button. Screenshot 2 (same package now ALSO served by a local `file:` apt repo + `apt-get update`, same running process, same Settings window re-opened): "Update to v0.9.1 available — Managed by apt (from file:/tmp/fakeppa jammy/main arm64 Packages) — update it the same way, not in-app: sudo apt update && sudo apt install --only-upgrade clipnest" + NO install button. Checksum-rejection proof (same container-local `/root/verify` copy, `LinuxAppUpdater.releasesAPIURL` temporarily pointed at a local `python3 -m http.server` serving a fake release+asset+checksum — again never the host tree): a stubbed `/usr/bin/pkexec` (logs every invocation to a file, then execs the real one) recorded ZERO invocations when one byte of the served `.deb` was corrupted in place (checksum file left pointing at the original good bytes) — outcome `.failed(.checksumMismatch)`, steps stopped at `.verifyingChecksum`, never reaching `.installing`; re-run against the unmodified good file reached `.installing` and DID invoke the real `pkexec` (logged: `apt-get install -y /tmp/clipnest-update-<uuid>-clipnest_9.9.9_arm64-jammy.deb` — exactly `pkexecAptGetInstallArguments`'s real output), which then failed with a REAL, SURFACED error ("Error getting authority: Error initializing authority: Could not connect: No such file or directory" — this bare container has no running polkit authority), returned as `.failed(.installationFailed(...))`, never silently swallowed. `pkexec` unattended-authentication is confirmed the expected, unexercisable case per the task's own framing — what's proven instead is that the correct command+arguments are constructed and that failure surfaces honestly.
  Unverified/disclosed: no real Linux `.deb`+`.sha256` release asset exists yet on the actual `AayushGour/clipnest` GitHub release (checked live: `v0.9.1`'s only assets are the macOS `.dmg`+`.dmg.sha256` — `release-linux.yml` hasn't run against a real push yet), so `selectDebAsset`/the full download path against the REAL repo is untested against a REAL asset — proven instead against a local fake server with the exact naming/checksum-file shape `release-linux.yml` itself produces. Found, not fixed (out of this task's owned-files scope — `debian/control` isn't in it): `debian/control`'s `clipnest` package `Depends:` list has no `curl` entry, and this repo's own `swift:6.0-jammy` build image doesn't ship `curl` by default either — if a real Ubuntu install genuinely lacks `/usr/bin/curl`, BOTH `UpdateChecker` (pre-existing) and this task's `LinuxAppUpdater` silently no-op (the existing `Process.run()` throw → `nil` → "no update found" path), never crash, but the "automatically check for updates" feature would be dead on such a machine; flagging for architect/PM, not silently patching a packaging manifest outside scope. Real GitHub-account pkexec/polkit-agent-present authentication was not exercisable in any container available this session (no real login session/seat) — disclosed, not assumed passing. `git add` done for `Sources/ClipnestLinuxAppKit/App/LinuxAppUpdater.swift`, `Sources/ClipnestGTK/Window/SettingsWindow{+General,}.swift`, `Sources/ClipnestLinuxAppKit/App/LinuxAppEnvironment.swift`, `Tests/ClipnestPlatformLinuxTests/{AppLinuxAppUpdaterTests,GTKUpdateSettingsPresentationTests}.swift`, `docs/API-ClipnestGTK.md`, `docs/API-ClipnestLinuxAppKit.md` (new) — no commit. Decision D88 in project-context.md. Dev ceiling `status:test` per integrity rule 2 (no separate reviewer/tester spawned this session, same disclosed precedent as T-OPT3/T-RTF1/T-KBPARITY2/3) — board updated directly (this session's own pen-holder, no spawner) → handing to reviewer then tester next. Mandatory security pass still owed: this task downloads+installs software and shells to a privileged helper (`pkexec`), squarely inside coding-standards.md's "external I/O"/"user input"-adjacent security-trigger scope — flagging explicitly, not skipping silently.
- 2026-09-08 [T-WB1-MITIGATE] Routed follow-up: mitigate T-WB1's Ubuntu 22.04 X11 CLIPBOARD-ownership SIGSEGV (GTK <4.10's NULL-unsafe `g_str_equal` in `gdk_x11_clipboard_request_targets_got_stream`) since T-WB1-GTKBUMP's "bump the dependency" ask has no real path: checked Launchpad's API directly (official archive/backports pockets top out at 4.6.9 for jammy; `~ricotz/staging` and `~gnome3-team/gnome3-staging`, the two most plausible GNOME-stack PPAs, carry zero `gtk4` sources in any status) — no credible backport exists. Built detection instead: `Sources/ClipnestGTK/Support/GTKClipboardCrashNotice.swift` (new — `GTKRuntimeVersion.isAffectedByX11ClipboardCrash` = `(major, minor) < (4, 10)`, pure, unit-tested with injected jammy/noble/boundary versions) + `Sources/ClipnestGTK/Interop/GTKClipboardCrashNoticeDetection.swift` (new — live `gtk_get_major/minor/micro_version()` read, the RUNTIME version, never the compile-time `GTK_MAJOR_VERSION` macros). X11-backend detection is a REAL `GDK_IS_X11_DISPLAY(gdk_display_get_default())` query (`clipnest_gdk_display_is_x11`, new in `Sources/CGdkX11/shim.h`, mirroring that file's existing `clipnest_gtk_window_set_x11_utility_type_hint` shape exactly) — deliberately NOT `SessionType`'s `XDG_SESSION_TYPE`/`WAYLAND_DISPLAY` env-var heuristic (used elsewhere for XTEST gating), because `deepwiki` against `GNOME/gtk` confirmed a Wayland session can still fall back to GDK's X11 backend if the Wayland backend fails to init, or `GDK_BACKEND=x11` is forced — an env-var-only check would be a real false negative for exactly the sessions this notice exists to catch. Surfaced as a new conditionally-built section in the Permissions tab (`SettingsWindow+Permissions.swift`) — built once in `buildPermissionsTab()` (this fact cannot change during the process's lifetime, unlike the uinput-grant state above it, so nothing here needs `refreshPermissionsStatus()`'s re-check-on-every-`show()` treatment). `SettingsWindow.init` gained a new required, non-defaulted `gtkClipboardCrashNoticeInfo: GTKClipboardCrashNoticeInfo` parameter (coding-standards.md's cross-platform-seam rule — a default would let the wire-up silently go missing, exactly the `presentSnippetEditor`/`openSettings` class of bug this session has hit repeatedly); `Sources/ClipnestLinuxAppKit/App/LinuxAppEnvironment.swift` resolves the real value ONCE via `GTKClipboardCrashNoticeDetection.detectCurrent()`, called after `ClipnestGTKApplication.initializeGTK()` (confirmed via `LinuxAppLifecycle.run()`'s real call order).

  Packaging (`debian/control`): left `Depends`/`Recommends` untouched on purpose. A versioned `Depends: libgtk-4-1 (>= 4.10)` or a `Breaks: libgtk-4-1 (<< 4.10)` would make `clipnest` UNINSTALLABLE on jammy outright (confirmed above: nothing in the archives or any backport satisfies it) — strictly worse than a working install with a rare, now-explained, self-healing crash (SQLite store survives intact per T-WB1's own container verification). A `Recommends` on the same permanently-unsatisfiable version is pure apt noise with no path forward. Added a plain-language caveat paragraph to `clipnest`'s Description instead — a documentation-only channel (`apt show clipnest`, pre-install-visible) alongside the runtime notice, which stays the primary mitigation. Full reasoning: `debian/README.source`'s "Known gap #5" UPDATE section (this task).

- 2026-09-08 [T-PRIM1/T-FLAKE1] Two independent tasks, one session. **T-PRIM1 (research, no code):** decided NOT to build PRIMARY-selection capture — a well-argued no, not a board-driven build. Field research via deepwiki against real source (Pano/GPaste/CopyQ, the same 3 projects competitive-analysis.md already primary-source-verified): all 3 gate PRIMARY behind an opt-in setting, default off, AND all 3 debounce/coalesce before persisting (Pano 500ms debounce merged into one history; GPaste a fully separate history track; CopyQ ~50ms + incomplete-selection detection + top-item-extend merge) — unanimous convergence, not an isolated caution. POC in own container `clipnest-prim-poc` (swift:6.0-jammy + Xvfb + xdotool + xclip + a C harness calling `XFixesQueryExtension`/`XFixesSelectSelectionInput` exactly as `X11State.swift` already does): confirmed the mechanism works for PRIMARY (atom interns fine, `xclip -o -selection primary` matched a real xdotool drag-selection). Measured real event volume rather than assuming it: one continuous 30-step drag = 1 `XFixesSelectionNotify` (ownership changes once per gesture, not per pixel — softer than the naive "floods on every drag" framing); 6 separate double-click selections = 10 events; 9 separate full press-drag-release cycles = 9 events — i.e. ~1 event per discrete selection action. The real risk this surfaces is qualitative, not volumetric: most selections are read/highlight, not copy-intent, so even a modest per-minute cadence would flood retention-limited History with noise, directly undercutting Clipnest's own "remembers exactly as much as you tell it to" positioning. Code-level finding beyond the board line's "cheap, same mechanism" framing: `X11State`/`X11ClipboardConnection` hardcode ONE `clipboardAtom`/reply-property/`pendingConversion` slot and `handleXFixesSelectionNotify` never inspects `fixesEvent.selection` — adding PRIMARY to the XFixes registration alone would NOT capture PRIMARY content, only spuriously re-trigger CLIPBOARD's own `checkNow()`. Real support needs a parameterized/parallel conversion pipeline plus a second changeCount concept, since `ClipboardMonitor`/`ClipItem`/`ClipStore` (`ClipnestCore`, shared with macOS, which has no PRIMARY concept at all) are single-stream abstractions with zero "origin selection" notion — a schema/cross-cutting decision, not a one-atom addition; correctly belongs with architect if/when prioritized, not built freehand under a P3 senior-dev task. Also found and reported (not fixed, out of my file scope): `ShellHelperClipboardSelection.primary`/`setClipboardWatch(includePrimary:)` already exist end-to-end in `ClipnestLinuxAppKit`'s D-Bus layer but are wired to zero call sites — a dormant, correctly-opt-in hook on the Shell-extension path, a better eventual home for this than extending the always-on default X11-bridge watcher. **T-FLAKE1 (fixed):** `DBusShellHelperClipboardClientTests.readClipboardClosesFileDescriptorOnInvalidReplyShape` asserted `fcntl(readEnd, F_GETFD) == -1` on a raw fd NUMBER — a process-global namespace swift-testing's concurrent suite execution shares, so a coincidental fd-number reuse by another suite between the real `close()` and this assertion misreads "closed" as "still open." Fixed by asking the pipe itself instead of the fd table: `poll()` the test's own `writeEnd` for `POLLERR`, which POSIX guarantees once a pipe's only reader is gone and reports unconditionally in `revents` — a kernel object private to this one test, immune to what any other concurrent test does with fd numbers; no `SIGPIPE` handling needed since no `write()` runs; reuses the exact `pollfd`/`POLLIN`-family Glibc pattern `X11ClipboardConnection.runEventLoop` already uses in production. Verified the fix isn't just "always green" via a container-local-only round trip (never touched the host tree): reintroducing the original leak (removing production's `close()` call) made the new assertion correctly FAIL; restoring production code made it pass again. Verification (own container `clipnest-prim1-dev`, bind-mounted `swift:6.0-jammy`, own build-cache volume; full-tree builds run against a container-local `/root/verify` snapshot because 2 other agents had transiently-broken, actively-in-flight WIP on `ClipnestGTK`/`ClipnestLinuxAppKit` hotkey files at snapshot time — a single container-local, never-host, stub property (`SettingsWindow+Shortcuts.swift`'s missing `reinstallExpandSnippetHotkeyFloor`) was added purely to unblock compiling the REST of the tree, confirmed via `git status`/`git diff --stat` on the host that zero of it leaked): Linux `swift test` **1267/1267** across 5 consecutive runs (3x default, 2x `--parallel`), plus 5/5 isolated-suite runs of the target suite alone; macOS `swift test` exact **605 tests / 51 suites**, unaffected (Linux-only test file); `./scripts/lint.sh Sources Tests` exit 0, re-executed inside pinned `swift:6.0-jammy` (confirmed via its own version-mismatch/re-exec log lines, not just trusted). Only file touched: `Tests/ClipnestPlatformLinuxTests/DBusShellHelperClipboardClientTests.swift` — `git add`ed, no commit. Decision D92 in `.claude/project-context.md`. Dev ceiling `status:test` for T-FLAKE1 (integrity rule 2, no separate reviewer/tester spawned this session); T-PRIM1 closed `status:done` directly since it produced no code, same precedent as T-CA2 (non-code research deliverable, evidence is the artifact itself). Both containers (`clipnest-prim-poc`, `clipnest-prim1-dev`) and the build-cache volume removed after verification.

`GlobalHotkeyRecorder` (unchanged validation/persistence machinery from T-OPT2, `GlobalHotkeyAcceleratorValidation` reused verbatim per the routed instruction) is now instantiated once per hotkey and cross-links to its sibling via a `weak var otherRecorder` so starting a NEW recording cancels an in-progress one on the OTHER row — two independent window-level `GtkEventControllerKey`s each gated on their own `isRecording` would otherwise race for the same next keypress with no defined winner. `LinuxShortcutDescriptions.swift`: doc-comment-only update (plural "two GLOBAL hotkeys," was singular). **Cross-module wiring this task cannot make itself** (`SettingsWindow.swift`/`LinuxAppEnvironment.swift` are outside this task's owned-files grant, same class of gap T-OPT2 hit for the first hotkey): `SettingsWindow` needs one new, non-defaulted stored closure `let reinstallExpandSnippetHotkeyFloor: (_ accelerator: String) -> Void` (mirrors `reinstallToggleHotkeyFloor` exactly — added as a SEPARATE closure, not a widened `(key, accelerator)` signature on the existing one, to avoid touching that closure's one already-wired call site for an unrelated reason) declared after line 89, threaded through `init` after the `reinstallToggleHotkeyFloor` parameter (after line 237) and its assignment (after line 256);
