- 2026-09-08 [T-ATSPI1] AT-SPI snippet-expansion tier had NEVER run against a real bus (every file in `Sources/ClipnestPlatformLinux/Accessibility/**` said "manual-verify only"). **Research**: read the existing `ATSPIFocusTracker`/`ATSPITextAccessor`/`DBusConnection`/`AccessibilityBusResolver` — wiring in `LinuxAppEnvironment.makeSelectedTextAccessing()` was already structurally correct (degrades to `NullSelectedTextAccessing` on any failure, never a silent default). Fetched the real `at-spi2-core` `EditableText.xml`/`Text.xml` from GNOME's own GitLab (API, not scraped) to verify the contract from primary sources: `InsertText`'s `position` is a character offset, `length` is a UTF-8 BYTE count of `text` (matches `UTF8OffsetConversion` already); `GetSelection` has TWO SEPARATE `out` args (`startOffset`, `endOffset`), never one `(ii)` struct. **POC**: own container (`swift:6.0-jammy` + `dbus`/`at-spi2-core`/`xvfb`/`imagemagick`/`xclip`/`xdotool`, two-context `docker build --build-context repo=<real tree>` so no `.dockerignore`/`Package.swift` patch ever touched the host repo) — a throwaway `ATSPIProbe` executable target (container-local Package.swift patch only) linking the REAL `ATSPITextAccessor`/`ATSPIFocusTracker`/`DBusConnection`, and a real two-entry GTK4 C test app. Result: AT-SPI IS viable in a container — real bus resolves, real `Hello`/SASL handshake succeeds, real focus tracking works (one inherent, verified limitation: a focus event firing before the tracker's `AddMatch` registers is lost forever — D-Bus signals aren't queued for late subscribers; irrelevant in production since Clipnest's own tracker starts at ITS launch, well before the user later focuses a field). **Real bug found via `dbus-monitor` tap on the live a11y bus**: `GetSelection`'s actual wire reply is two top-level `int32`s (confirmed: `int32 7` then a separate `int32 13`), not the `.structure([.int32,.int32])` the existing `parseSelectionReply` (and its own unit test) assumed — this made the AT-SPI tier fail on literally EVERY real call, 0% success rate, always silently falling through to clipboard regardless of app. Fixed `Sources/ClipnestPlatformLinux/Accessibility/ATSPIRequests.swift`; re-verified the full round trip end-to-end through the REAL public `SnippetExpander.expand()` (not just the primitive) — GTK4 app's own `notify::text` log confirms the in-place replace landed correctly, clipboard sentinel set before the run survived byte-for-byte after, and a live multi-byte string (`"héllo wörld"`, 11 chars/13 bytes) confirmed `InsertText`'s length=13 on the real wire. Fixed with a new pure, unit-tested `AXSelectedTextAccessor.isTrustworthy(role:)` (mirrors `Paster.isStillFrontmost`'s extraction pattern) rejecting an `AXWebArea`-rooted read — confirmed live across 2 independent fresh Antigravity launches (guard fires, falls through to the clipboard path, clipboard still restored correctly; full match+replace success in Antigravity itself was blocked by a test-harness focus-targeting quirk in that unfamiliar IDE, not by Clipnest, since the fallback mechanics are the identical code already proven via the 11 VS Code trials). - 2026-09-12 [T-AXTRUST-LINUX1] Symmetric AT-SPI write verification (D97/D100), design-consistency task, no live phantom-write repro on Linux. **Measured, not assumed, the AT-SPI offset unit** — the routing brief's own hard requirement, given T-ATSPI1/D95's precedent of a wrong wire-format assumption slipping through because its own unit test encoded the same wrong assumption: own container (`clipnest-build:hk`, own name `axtrust-linux1-build`, disposable, removed after use), installed `xvfb`/`at-spi2-core`/`dbus-x11`/`libatspi2.0-dev`, launched a real session bus (`dbus-launch`) + real a11y bus (`at-spi-bus-launcher --launch-immediately`) + a hand-built minimal GTK4 `GtkEntry` app, driven via `python3-dbus` directly against the live `org.a11y.atspi.Text`/`EditableText` interfaces (no fakes). Two disambiguating probes: (1) an astral emoji ("a😀bcd") gave `Text.CharacterCount==5` and `GetText(1,2)` returned the WHOLE 4-byte emoji as ONE offset step — rules out UTF-16 code units (would be 6, split surrogate pair) and UTF-8 bytes (would be 8); (2) a base+combining-mark sequence ("e"+U+0301, 1 `Character`/grapheme, 2 scalars) gave `CharacterCount==6` for "a"+"é"+"bcd" with `GetText(1,2)`/`GetText(2,3)` returning the base letter and the combining mark SEPARATELY — rules out extended-grapheme-cluster counting (the same "a test that agrees with the assumption proves nothing" trap T-ATSPI1 named, since a plain "héllo"-style string can't distinguish scalar from grapheme count). Capped off with an actual `dbus-monitor --address unix:path=/root/.cache/at-spi/bus` capture (the session-bus-only `--session` tap does NOT see this traffic — a11y is a separate private bus, found the hard way) of a live `EditableText.InsertText(position: 1, text: "😀Y", length: 5)` call: `CharacterCount` advanced by exactly 2 (the scalar count) and the immediate `Text.GetText(1,3)` read back "😀Y" byte-for-byte. **Conclusion: AT-SPI Text/EditableText offsets are Unicode SCALAR (codepoint) offsets** — confirms `UTF8OffsetConversion.swift`'s pre-existing doc comment claim, now with real teeth, not just an unverified assertion. Implementation: `ATSPITextAccessor.replaceSelectedText` no longer trusts a `true` `InsertText` reply at face value — after it, reads back `GetText(selection.start, selection.start + UTF8OffsetConversion.scalarCount(of: text))` (new pure helper, mirrors `utf8ByteCount`, unit-tested) and compares to `text`; any mismatch or unreadable range reports `false`. **Critically, this does NOT run the existing best-effort rollback** — added an explicit doc-comment section explaining why: the rollback fires from a KNOWN state (delete confirmed done, insert confirmed failed via a `false`/timeout reply), while a verified-but-suspicious insert is an UNKNOWN state (the reply already lied once), so re-inserting `originalText` on top of it risks duplicating content instead of recovering it — reports failure and lets `SnippetExpander`'s clipboard tier (re-reads live state) take over instead. Explicitly honest in the doc comment and here: no live AT-SPI phantom-write repro exists — this is contract symmetry (D100), not an observed defect, unlike the macOS AX phantom-write bug it mirrors. New tests: `replacePhantomInsertReturnsFalseWithoutRollback` (true reply, disagreeing read-back → false, exactly 1 InsertText call, rollback never attempted), `replaceUnverifiableInsertReturnsFalseWithoutRollback` (verification read itself times out → same), `scalarCountMatchesUnicodeScalarView`/`scalarCountDiffersFromCharacterCountForCombiningSequences` for the new helper; updated `replaceDeletesThenInserts`'s fake (`FakeATSPIObjectCalling` gained a `queuedRepliesByMember` FIFO-per-member mechanism, since `GetText` is now called twice per successful flow for two different purposes) to also assert the verification call's exact scalar-offset body. **Verification, isolated from the shared tree's concurrent WIP** (branch was found switched to `linux-migration` mid-task with another agent's uncommitted macOS D97 work present — same shared-tree hazard this board has flagged repeatedly, not mine to fix): `git worktree` at HEAD `622b02a`, my 4-file diff applied via `git apply`, copied into the disposable container. `swift build --target ClipnestPlatformLinux` clean. `swift test`: base (unpatched, same commit) **1270**, patched **1274** — exactly +4 net new, 0 regressions, matches the board's stated baseline. `./scripts/lint.sh Sources Tests` (run on the real host tree, re-executed inside pinned `swift:6.0-jammy` per its own re-exec logic) → exit 0, clean. macOS `swift test` (this Mac, real Apple Swift 6.3.0 toolchain, real tree) → **"Test run with 605 tests in 51 suites passed"**, exact, unaffected (Linux-only files, `#if os(Linux)`-gated). `docs/API-ClipnestPlatformLinux.md` updated in the same change: a new "Write re-verification" bullet under `replaceSelectedText` and a new "measured against a real bus" paragraph under "Verified behavior" documenting both disambiguating probes + the `dbus-monitor` capture — did not touch `docs/features.md`'s snippet-expansion section (that section is macOS-AX-specific and is the concurrent T-AXTRUST1/macOS agent's own doc slice; Linux behavior's dedicated home is `API-ClipnestPlatformLinux.md`, already updated). Did not touch `ClipnestApp/**`, `Sources/ClipnestCore/Paste/SnippetExpander.swift`, or any shared protocol, per this task's explicit file boundaries. Cleaned up: removed my own container (`axtrust-linux1-build`) and both scratch `git worktree`s after use — did not touch any of the ~26 pre-existing containers from other agents. `git add`ed exactly `Sources/ClipnestPlatformLinux/Accessibility/{ATSPITextAccessor,UTF8OffsetConversion}.swift`, `Tests/ClipnestPlatformLinuxTests/{ATSPITextAccessorTests,ATSPIUTF8OffsetConversionTests}.swift`, `docs/API-ClipnestPlatformLinux.md`.
